```text
52	## Your Lore
53	
54	Your lore is stored in the `lore/` directory within your agent directory. It is a collection of plain markdown files — each file is a **lore topic**, an atomic piece of knowledge.
55	
56	Lore topics contain:
57	- Decisions and their reasoning
58	- Domain knowledge and discoveries
59	- Operational recommendations from experience
60	- Practical context about the systems and environment you work in
61	
62	Lore topics reference each other by filename, forming a knowledge graph. Some topics are **summary topics** that provide an overview of an area and link to more specific topics.
63	
64	### Searching Your Lore
65	
66	`lore-context.md` is a compressed index, not the full picture — treat it as a starting point, not the answer. At the start of any non-trivial task, scan your `lore/` directory for related topics before proceeding. Never act on assumptions about things you might have encountered in previous sessions without first confirming in your lore.
67	
68	Default lore search means `lore/` only. Do **not** search `sessions/` or `archive/` as part of ordinary recall: `sessions/` contains summaries, and `archive/` contains full session logs that can be overwhelmingly large. Search or read `archive/` only when a human explicitly asks about full session logs / a particular session, or when you have a concrete reason that session-log evidence is important for the task.
69	
70	When you need to search or recall lore, read `<framework-root>/docs/lore-search.md` and follow the procedure there.
71	
72	## Your Workdir
73	
74	Your agent directory contains a `workdir/` directory. This is your persistent workspace for files, scripts, tools, and any other artifacts you create or need across sessions.
75	
76	You decide the internal structure of `workdir/` — organize it however makes sense for your work.
77	
78	## Workspace Visibility
79	
80	You have access to the entire workspace — all sibling repositories, data, and resources. Your lore is specific to you, but your reach is workspace-wide.
81	
82	## Collaborating with Other Agents
83	
84	The user may invoke any of three cross-agent mechanisms during the session:
85	
86	- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore across the **currently loaded** agents (you, plus any attached guests). Fans out to one subagent per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/recall.md` and `lore-search.md`.
87	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — ask an **unloaded** agent a focused question. A subagent boots the consultant, answers, and exits. You get back a synthesis plus pointers to specific lore topics or workdir tools you can read or use directly. No finalization for the consultant. See `<framework-root>/docs/consult.md`.
88	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a **guest** into this session for sustained co-work. You remain the sole executor (host); the guest's role and lore-context join yours. Subsequent recalls fan out to the guest too, and finalization iterates per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/attach.md`.
89	
90	Rough rule: recall is for lore you already have loaded; consult is a one-shot question with file handover; attach is for sustained multi-turn work spanning multiple agents' knowledge.
91	
92	## Session Finalization
93	
94	At the end of a session, when the user triggers finalization, you preserve what you learned. This is a two-step process:
95	
96	1. **Reflection** — extract what's worth keeping into reflection topics. Triggered by `/lr:reflect`.
97	2. **Merge** — a separate step integrates reflections into your lore. Triggered by `/lr:merge`.
98	
99	Both steps together: `/lr:finalize`.
100	
101	If guests are attached to this session (via `/lr:attach`), both reflection and merge iterate per active agent in host-first order — each agent learns what fits its role. See `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and `process-merge.md` for the iteration mechanics.
102	
103	Do not perform finalization unless the user explicitly triggers it.
```

```text
8	## Binding values
9	
10	| Binding | Value on Claude Code |
11	|---|---|
12	| **framework-root** | Self-locate (Step 0). `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` also expands to it and may be used as a literal-path fallback. |
13	| **invocation-syntax** | Skills are user-invoked as slash commands `/lr:<skill>`; the engine expands them. Canonical skill folders live under `skills/<skill>/`. Per-agent boot shortcuts `/lr-<agent>-agent`. |
14	| **subagent-spawn** | The `Agent` tool. Fan-out = **N parallel `Agent` calls in a single message**. Sub-agent types: `general-purpose` (write), `Explore` (read-only, but excerpt-based — it locates material rather than reviewing it in depth), and `fork` (**inherits the caller's full conversation context**). Each subagent boots as its target agent and reads the procedure doc itself. Two traps: (1) `fork` is unsuitable wherever a subagent must be *independent* of the caller's reasoning — it carries the caller's context by design; (2) passing a **`name`** makes the call an Agent-Teams teammate, and **a teammate does not auto-return its final report to the caller** — only an unnamed call does. Spawn unnamed when you need the result back, or instruct a named teammate to `SendMessage` its report before going idle. |

16	| **runtime-bounding** | The Bash-tool `timeout` parameter bounds a command's runtime. |
```

```text
1	# Auto-Pull (Single-Repo Refresh)
2	
3	> **Audience note.** This is an internal procedure doc — Claude reads it from `agent-boot.md`, `attach.md`, `process-merge.md`, and `pull-lore.md`. There is no `/lr:auto-pull` skill. Users invoke its behavior implicitly via boot/attach/merge or explicitly via `/lr:pull-lore`.
4	
5	The shared procedure for refreshing one lore agent repo's git state mid-flow. Used by:
6	
7	- **`agent-boot.md` step 2** — auto-pull the host repo at boot time.
8	- **`attach.md`** — auto-pull the guest repo before reading its role/lore-context.
9	- **`process-merge.md` step 0** — defense-in-depth pull right before the merge subagent reads its lore.
10	- **`docs/pull-lore.md`** — the user-invoked `/lr:pull-lore` skill iterates this procedure across active agents.
11	
12	The procedure is intentionally narrow: one `git pull --ff-only` against the agent's repo, with safety gates and a degraded-mode failure path. No clone logic (that's `/lr:workspace-pull`), no migration logic (that's `version-check.md`), no commit or push.
13	
14	## Inputs
15	
16	- `<lore-agent-repo>` — absolute path to the lore agent repo to refresh.
17	
18	## Procedure
19	
20	Run all git invocations with `git -C <lore-agent-repo> ...` — never `cd` into the repo (other tools share the shell working directory; see `conventions.md` § Tooling: CWD Safety).
21	
22	### Step 1: Skip non-git or remote-less repos
23	
24	Run `git -C <lore-agent-repo> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`. If it fails (not a git repo), skip with a one-line note: `<lore-agent-repo>: not a git repo — skipping auto-pull` and return successfully (the rest of the flow continues in degraded mode).
25	
26	Run `git -C <lore-agent-repo> remote get-url origin`. If it fails (no `origin` remote configured), skip with: `<lore-agent-repo>: no origin remote — skipping auto-pull` and return successfully.
27	
28	These are not failures — they're legitimate states (e.g., a brand-new local-only agent repo, or an unusual remote layout). Auto-pull simply has nothing to do.
29	
30	### Step 2: Pull
31	
32	Run `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 git -C <lore-agent-repo> pull --ff-only`, bounded to roughly 60 seconds.
33	
34	> **Engine note.** The bounding mechanism below is Claude Code's Bash-tool timeout. On other engines, follow your profile's **runtime-bounding** binding (`docs/engines/<engine>.md`) — e.g. on Codex there is no Bash-tool timeout flag; the sandbox / `job_max_runtime_seconds` bounds it, and the "set the Bash-tool timeout" prose is ignored.
35	
36	**Apply the timeout via your Bash tool's own timeout parameter — do *not* wrap the command in a `timeout`/`gtimeout` binary.** Those are GNU coreutils tools, absent by default on macOS/BSD (the primary dev platform); `timeout 60 git …` fails with `command not found` (exit 127), which aborts the pull and drops boot into degraded mode for an entirely spurious reason — silently disabling auto-pull on every macOS boot. See `conventions.md` § Tooling: Portable Shell.
37	
38	The **Bash-tool timeout is the transport-agnostic backstop** — it kills any stall (SSH, HTTPS, or a future remote type) so boot can never hang indefinitely, however the remote authenticates. The env vars below are per-transport *fast-fail* niceties layered on top, so a stall errors out in seconds rather than waiting out the full timeout:
39	
40	- **`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`** — stops Git prompting on the terminal for **HTTP(S)** credentials, so an HTTPS pull without cached credentials fails fast (~0.5s) instead of blocking on a username prompt. It governs only Git's *own* terminal prompt — a separate GUI credential helper (e.g. Git Credential Manager) or the `ssh` binary is unaffected and falls to the backstop.
41	- **`GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10'`** (**SSH** only) — `BatchMode=yes` turns an unknown host key or passphrase prompt into an immediate failure; `ConnectTimeout` bounds the TCP connect. No effect on HTTPS remotes.
42	- *(optional, **HTTPS**)* add `-c http.lowSpeedLimit=1000 -c http.lowSpeedTime=15` **before the `pull` subcommand** (e.g. `git -c http.lowSpeedLimit=1000 -c http.lowSpeedTime=15 -C <lore-agent-repo> pull --ff-only`) — aborts a connected-but-stalled transfer after ~15s under ~1 KB/s, the rough analog of SSH's `ConnectTimeout`. The backstop already covers this case; add it only if you want a faster, cleaner abort.
43	- **`--ff-only`** ensures divergent local branches surface as failures rather than producing silent merge commits.
44	
45	**Why we don't gate on a dirty working tree:** `git pull --ff-only` is non-writing in spirit — it advances `HEAD`, but it refuses cleanly if the fast-forward would clobber any uncommitted edits in the working tree. So uncommitted edits are either preserved through the pull or the pull fails with a clear error. Either outcome is safe. Gating on dirty would defeat the most useful invocation site (pre-merge auto-pull, where `reflections/` from phase 1 is intentionally uncommitted).
46	
47	This is **different** from the version-check upgrade gate (`docs/version-check.md`), which *does* refuse on dirty — because version-check *writes* to `lore-repo.md`. Auto-pull doesn't write to file contents.
48	
49	### Step 3: Report
50	
51	The verbosity rule depends on the calling site. Boot/attach/merge are quiet on the no-op case (the common path) so the surrounding flow stays uncluttered; `/lr:pull-lore` is always verbose because it's user-invoked.
52	
53	| Outcome | Boot / attach / merge | `/lr:pull-lore` |
54	|---|---|---|
55	| Already up to date | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: already up to date` |
56	| Fast-forwarded | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` |
57	| Skipped (not a git repo / no origin) | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: skipped — <reason>` |
58	| Failed (non-FF, network, auth) | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` |
59	
60	For the commit count, `git rev-list HEAD@{1}..HEAD --count` works after a fast-forward; if it fails or returns 0 unexpectedly, just print `pulled` without a count.
61	
62	Never abort the surrounding flow on a pull failure. The agent must always finish booting / attaching / merging even if its repo is momentarily out of sync.
63	
64	## Worktrees
65	
66	The lore framework's worktree convention (see `worktrees.md`) keeps top-level repo checkouts on their default branch and pushes feature work into `<workspace>/.worktrees/<repo>/<slug>/`. Auto-pull operates on whichever path was passed in — the path discovered by boot/attach.
67	
68	- **Booted from a top-level checkout:** auto-pull fast-forwards the default branch as expected. This is the common path.
69	- **Booted from inside a worktree** (the discovered path resolves under `.worktrees/`): auto-pull runs against that worktree's checkout. If the worktree is on a feature branch with no upstream, or the upstream has diverged, the pull fails with a clear error and the surrounding flow continues in degraded mode. Manage feature-branch updates manually — auto-pull is best-effort.
70	
71	`/lr:pull-lore` and the boot/attach/merge sites all delegate to this same procedure, so the worktree behavior is uniform.
72	
73	## Invariants
74	
75	- **Best-effort.** A pull failure never blocks boot, attach, merge, or any user-visible flow. The surrounding flow continues in degraded mode with a visible warning.
76	- **No destructive actions.** Never `--force`, never `git reset`, never `git checkout` away from the user's branch. If a fast-forward isn't possible, the failure surfaces and the user resolves manually.
77	- **Uncommitted changes are preserved.** `--ff-only` either advances `HEAD` cleanly past the dirty tree or refuses with a clear error — both outcomes are safe. Auto-pull never stashes, resets, or otherwise touches the working tree.
78	- **Per-repo scope.** Auto-pull operates on a single repo at a time. Multi-repo flows (e.g., `/lr:pull-lore` over host + guests) iterate this procedure per repo.
79	- **Idempotent.** Running auto-pull twice in a row on a clean repo produces no observable difference past the first run.
80	
81	## Distinct From
82	
83	- **`/lr:workspace-pull`** — filesystem-wide: discovers `lore-repo.md` files, clones missing repos declared in `repos:`, pulls every top-level git repo. Auto-pull is single-repo and never clones.
84	- **`version-check.md`** — repo-version reconciliation: applies migrations and stamps `lore-repo.md` after a framework `VERSION` bump. Auto-pull is git-only and never modifies file contents.
85	- **`resolve-conflicts.md`** — finalize-time merge of remote conflicts in agent subtrees. Triggered only when push is rejected; auto-pull is the *prevention* arm of the same problem.
86	
87	The three sit on a spectrum: auto-pull keeps the local repo fresh (so the rest of the flow reads current data); `/lr:workspace-pull` keeps the workspace fresh (so all repos are present and up to date); resolve-conflicts heals after a concurrent finalize collided. They compose without overlap.
88	
89	## See Also
90	
91	- `pull-lore.md` — the `/lr:pull-lore` skill that user-invokes this procedure mid-session.
92	- `agent-boot.md` — boot-time invocation site.
93	- `attach.md` — guest-attach invocation site.
```

```text
9	## Responsibilities

20	## Three-Layer Design Model (v11+)
21	
22	Framework changes now sort into three discrete layers, each with its own vocabulary, skills, and lifecycle. When designing a feature, ask which layer owns it:
23	
24	1. **Plugin layer** — `lore-framework/` itself: skills, docs, migrations, scripts, marketplace config, `VERSION`. Distributed via marketplace; one source of truth across all installs.
25	2. **Domain layer** — a single agent repo's identity and scope: `lore-repo.md` schema (description, version, `repos:`), `agents/` directory shape, conventions for what an agent repo carries. "Domain" is the conceptual scope of one agent repo.
26	3. **Workspace layer** — the filesystem context where Claude runs: discovery across `lore-workspace.md` plus multiple `lore-repo.md` files, cross-repo orchestration (`/lr:workspace-pull`), setup/refresh (`/lr:workspace-init`), `<workspace>` placeholder. The workspace can hold one or more agent repos plus their declared sibling repos.
27	
28	`/lr:workspace-sync` was the first skill operating purely at the workspace layer; v25 renamed and expanded it into `/lr:workspace-pull` with two-level repo declarations. See `architecture-overview.md`, `workspace-vs-domain-vocabulary.md`, `v25-workspace-pull-init-design.md`.
29

31	- Workspace contents → `lore-workspace.md`, domain `repos:`, `/lr:workspace-pull`, and `/lr:workspace-init`.
32	- Agent repo identity/scope → `lore-repo.md` frontmatter, conceptually domain-level.
33	- Individual agent behavior → `role.md` / `lore-context.md` / lore topics.
34	
35	## Lore-Curation Disciplines
36	
37	Operational disciplines the architect applies during finalization, especially the finalizations that ship a `VERSION` bump:
38	
39	- **Version-history backfill (v12+)** — at every finalization that lands a `VERSION` bump, check whether `versioning-release-types.md` has an entry for the new version. If not, add it: kind (migration / release-notes / both), scope summary, and **cache-affecting?** annotation. Backfill on the same finalization as the ship — never "we'll get to it later." Drift in the history list erodes the topic's value as a per-version classification index. See `versioning-release-types.md`, `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`.
40	- **Cache-clear-footer authoring (v12+)** — when authoring release notes for a cache-affecting version (touches `skills/`, `scripts/`, or any SKILL.md-referenced doc whose runtime behavior changes), include the Clear Plugin Cache footer per the canonical wording in `lore-framework/docs/conventions.md`. Hoist the section near the top, not the bottom. See `cache-clear-footer-convention.md`.
41	- **Plugin-manifest-version bump (v14+; four version-bearing manifests since v25)** — at every finalization that lands a `VERSION` bump, set `version` to `1.<VERSION>.0` in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (the `lr` entry), `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json`, and `.codex-plugin/plugin.json`. Claude manifests are the original cache-detection lever; Cursor is consistency/visibility hygiene; Codex is the version native installs read. The mapping is mechanical, can't be forgotten. `/lr:check` #19 enforces it. See `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`, `consistency-checks.md`.
42	- **Name foundational principles as topics (meta-rule)** — when designing a feature and the rationale traces back to an unnamed framing, name the framing as its own lore topic before continuing. See `naming-foundational-principles.md`.
43	- **Don't defer completable scope** — bounded mechanical sweeps (terminology fixes, history backfills, broken-link cleanups) belong in the current ship, not "vN.1 follow-up." See `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`.
44	- **Multi-round multi-lens pre-ship review (v15+; instrument shipped v30)** — for any release that touches procedural docs (skill bodies, shared-procedure docs, conventions, migrations), run multi-lens review iteratively until a round produces zero findings worth fixing. That convergence is the ship signal. Single-pass review tends to miss "fix-the-fixes" surface bugs that emerge as drift between adjacent sites; v15 took 7 rounds (exceptional, due to four-task scope), typical doc-only releases converge in 2–3. **The discipline is unchanged; its execution now goes through `/lr:trilens-loop`** rather than hand-assembling the fan-out — the skill enforces what a hand-run pass forgets (cold-context reviewer independence, the APPLIED/DECLINED ledger that stops settled findings resurfacing, the "a silent round is not a clean round" guard, disclosure of any rail switched off) and routes the spawn through the engine binding instead of leaving me to remember the named-teammate trap, which is exactly how I hit it. Keep judging *which* lenses fit the change yourself — that judgement stays in lore. See `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`.
45	- **A gate result belongs to a specific artifact state (v30+)** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the artifact they actually ran against. An edit landed after the gates pass is ungated work: either re-run the affected gate or revert the edit and file the finding as follow-up — and never report "converged and green" for a tree neither gate saw. Record which commit / working-tree state a gate result belongs to, or the result silently migrates onto a different artifact. See `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md`.
46	- **Empirical pre-ship verification (v18+; sharpened v21/v28)** — for any release that changes a procedure doc covered by the lifecycle testing harness, run the lifecycle suite (`tests/lifecycle/`, real engine execution, complete scenario catalog per engine) — in addition to, not instead of, multi-lens prose review. Review catches structural/reasoning issues; the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to review by a strong model, because a strong-model reviewer resolves the same ambiguity a strong-model author wrote it under. **Pre-ship = pre-push, and the gate is the *complete* suite on the cheapest practical model per engine, not a proportionate subset or an accidental expensive tier:** Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5. Run it in the background, wait, push only on green. Proportionality reasoning ("this ship doesn't touch those procedures, so skip them") is a review-time judgment, not a substitute for the gate. See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `agent-boot-doc-fidelity-fixes.md`.
47	- **Sandboxed-review blind spot (v28+)** — a review environment that structurally blocks a capability doesn't just miss findings in that area, it can green-light code whose primary path never executed. Concrete instance: a Codex review pass on Lore Beings' Keeper ran in a sandbox that blocked `ps`, so every PID-identity check took the "unknown, can't verify" branch — the confirmed-match/confirmed-mismatch logic never ran once, the suite went green, and a real bug (`macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`) survived until a fourth pass on real, unsandboxed macOS. Before trusting a green suite, check whether the review environment has a known-blocked capability the code under test depends on; if so, either re-run in an environment where it works, or confirm via the test's own branching that the "unavailable" path isn't silently substituting for the real one. This extends the empirical-pre-ship-verification framing above with an orthogonal axis (capability availability, not model tier). One confirmed instance of the *false-green* form — see `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`; promote to its own lore topic (candidate name `sandbox-degraded-review-blind-spot.md`) on a second independent occurrence of that form, per the name-foundational-principles bar. A related but distinct form is now recorded: a capability revoked *mid-run* makes results **uninterpretable** rather than falsely green (`macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md`, v30) — same reflex (check the environment before believing the verdict), different failure mode, so it does not yet count as the second occurrence.
48	- **Standing improvement list maintenance (v27+)** — maintain `workdir/what-to-improve.md` as an always-current, prioritized action list for framework improvements, distinct from `framework-improvements-backlog.md` (the canonical unranked store with full detail). The standing list is the ranked action view: reread it at the start of every framework-work session, work it down, refresh it (re-rank, insert new findings, delete-don't-mark shipped/moot items) at each architecture review. User-established practice, 2026-07-18 — the explicit intent is that a list of this shape *always exists*, not that one gets produced once per review. See `standing-improvement-list-practice.md`.
49	
50	These disciplines compose: a version ship is the moment when (a) release notes get the cache-clear footer if cache-affecting, (b) `versioning-release-types.md` history gets the new entry with cache-affecting annotation, (c) all version-bearing plugin manifests get bumped to `1.<VERSION>.0`, (d) any newly-discovered foundational principle from the design discussion gets its own topic, and (e) touched procedures get their lifecycle scenarios run, not just reviewed.
51
```

```text
5	## Style Skills

7	A category of user-invoked `/lr:` skill that changes how the agent *communicates or collaborates* (vs. operations like `/lr:recall`/`/lr:merge`). 12	
13	## System Architecture
14	
15	Three discrete layers — identify which one owns a change before touching files:
16	1. **Plugin** (`lore-framework/`, installed as `lr`) — what's distributed via the marketplace: skills, docs, migrations, scripts, manifests, `VERSION`. Universal across installs.
17	2. **Domain** — the conceptual scope of one agent repo, marked by `lore-repo.md` (frontmatter: `description`, `version`, optional `repos:`). Holds `agents/<name>/` with `role.md`, `lore-context.md`, `lore/`, `workdir/`, `sessions/`.
18	3. **Workspace** — the filesystem Claude runs from; holds one or more agent repos + their declared siblings. Discovery scans workspace-root dirs for `lore-repo.md`; nested repos are invisible to most skills.
19	
20	See `architecture-overview.md`, `workspace-vs-domain-vocabulary.md`, `agent-discovery-nesting-constraint.md`, `plugin-vs-agent-repo-separation.md`.
21	
22	## Design Principles
23	
24	Identity-layer framings that frame everything else:
25	- **Team-shared knowledge** — agents are team-shared knowledge containers, not personal notebooks. Design for concurrent multi-contributor use.
26	- **Engine, not KB** — the framework is the engine/environment for self-improving agents; the knowledge base is a consequence, not the identity.
27	- **Executors first, advisors second** — primary value is getting things done; conversation is secondary. The usage→learning positive feedback loop only spins under executor-first framing.
28	
29	What each agent carries: **knowledge** (markdown — what it knows, accrues passively via reflection) + **skills** (tools + instructions — what it can do, evolves actively via in-flight teaching). Distinct assets; don't collapse them.
30	
31	Core mechanics: directory-driven, plain markdown (frontmatter only on descriptor files), git-as-metadata, delete-don't-mark, knowledge graph by filename reference, concise context with detail on demand, skill/doc separation, repo-level versioning. Framework owns the universal; agents own repo/host/workflow specifics.
32	
33	**Subagent as optimization vs subagent as semantics** — before letting any engine degrade a
34	subagent-spawning procedure to serial host-side execution, classify what the subagent is *for*. If it
35	buys parallelism and context isolation (recall, consult, attach, merge, conflict resolution),
36	serialization is lossless. If the subagent's *independence from the caller* is the deliverable
37	(`/lr:trilens-loop`), serialization destroys the feature — the procedure must stop and report. Profile
38	degradation clauses therefore need carve-outs, not blanket rules, and "the conservative profile passed
39	the whole suite" only ever meant it was adequate for the procedures that existed then. See
40	`subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`.
41	
42	See `system-design-principles.md` (the full list and the overreach diagnostics), plus the framing topics `team-shared-knowledge-principle.md`, `framework-as-engine-not-kb.md`, `agents-are-executors-first.md`, `knowledge-vs-skills-distinction.md`, `framework-scope-vs-agent-scope.md`.
43	
44	## Skills & Docs
45	
46	Operations are Claude Code plugin skills, `lr:` prefix on Claude; Cursor uses `/lr-<skill>` via
47	prefixed wrappers (`cursor-dual-skill-tree-one-repo.md`). **Skills are thin pointers** — each `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` is a one-line reference to `docs/<name>.md`, where all logic lives. Same for generated `/lr-<agent>-agent` boot commands (thin delegations to `agent-boot.md`). When a skill orchestrates sub-skills, the orchestration gets its own `docs/<skill>.md`; non-skill procedures shared across call sites get a `docs/<procedure>.md` (e.g. `auto-pull.md`). See `slash-command-system.md`, `skill-doc-pattern.md`, `shared-procedure-doc-pattern.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`.

Practical limit to remember before promising a long wait: on Claude Code a single MCP call dies at the engine's ~30-minute idle timeout, and the abort leaves `lr-wait`'s single-request lock stuck `busy` for the rest of the session — chunk waits at ≤29 min, or use a backgrounded shell timer. See `plugin-mcp-server-convention.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`.

51	## Engine Hubs
52	
53	Engine-specific operational knowledge now has one hub topic per engine: `claude-engine-capabilities.md`,
54	`codex-engine-capabilities.md`, and `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`. Use them as the entry points
55	for install/update model, invocation surface, subagent mechanism, memory file, MCP/plugin loading,
56	sandbox constraints, and lifecycle-harness caveats; keep atomic findings in the linked detailed
57	topics rather than rediscovering them from old session notes. Cursor live usage retrieval

61	`docs/engines/<engine>.md`'s binding — the doc read at the moment of use — not only in these hubs or in

64	§ Engine traps belong in the binding, and audit sibling profiles whenever one binding gains a guardrail.

67	
68	Shipping one repo to multiple engines' plugin marketplaces means handling **each engine's packaging
69	separately** — manifest schema, skill-tree location, and update model all differ, so Claude parity
70	does *not* imply Cursor/Codex parity. Claude Code is strict-clean; remaining public-distribution step
71	is Console-form community submission. Cursor is structurally ready, but seamless multi-user
72	propagation still needs a team marketplace + Auto Refresh + Cursor GitHub App validation. Codex
73	native packaging is resolved in v25: legacy Claude marketplace fallback still works, native

83	competitive landscape (`similar-projects-landscape.md`) before any positioning-sensitive

88	
89	Boot (`agent-boot.md`, single source of truth): discover agent → auto-pull repo → version check → read `role.md` + `lore-context.md` → detect teammate spawn → confirm. **Boot loads only those two files; topics are read on demand.** Repos auto-pull at every session-context boundary (boot, attach, pre-merge) to match the team's latest pushed state; `/lr:pull-lore` is the manual refresh. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md`, `auto-pull-mechanism.md`.
90	
91	## Cross-Agent Collaboration
92	
93	- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore of already-loaded agents (host + guests); fan-out per agent.
94	- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — one-shot question to an unloaded agent; a subagent boots it, answers with file pointers, exits.
95	- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a sustained guest; host stays sole executor, host-wins on conflicts.
96	- **`/lr:spawn-teammate` (BETA)** — spawn agents as Agent Teams teammates for parallel panes; the teammate's primary interlocutor is the user, not the lead.
97	
98	See `lore-search-pattern.md`, `consult-pattern.md`, `attach-pattern.md`, `spawn-teammate-feature.md`, `teammate-conventions.md`.

101	
102	**`/lr:takeover`** converts engine-native session logs into a markdown digest so a new session on any engine can continue interrupted work. Codex, Claude Code, and Cursor are supported (`scripts/session-takeover` — list, convert, render). Cursor pairs tool results from `store.db` to JSONL `tool_use` batches via batch-window name matching; same-name parallel batches and interrupted sessions set `pairing_uncertain`. See `takeover-feature.md`, `cursor-takeover-batch-pairing.md`, `engine-session-log-formats.md`.
103	
104	## Finalization
105	
106	User-triggered, four phases (`/lr:finalize` runs all; phases also run standalone): **reflect** (inline, host-first, per agent — needs session context) → **merge** (parallel subagents, one per agent booted as itself, file-driven — integrates reflections into `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, `role.md`) → **summarize** (host writes the canonical session summary + short guest summaries) → **commit+push** (one commit per touched repo; conflict-resolution on push rejection). Do not finalize unless the user triggers it. See `finalization-process.md`, `finalize.md`, `merge-in-booted-subagents.md`, `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md`.

109	
110	`lore-framework/VERSION` is the single source of truth; **the current shipped version is v30**. Each
111	agent repo stamps that version in its `lore-repo.md`, and four version-bearing plugin manifests mirror
112	`1.<VERSION>.0` (`/lr:check` #19 enforces). A version is either **migration**, **release-notes-only**,
113	or both, and independently **cache-affecting** or not — those two axes are orthogonal, and every ship
114	records both in `versioning-release-types.md`, which holds the full per-version history. Read that
115	topic for what any given version contained; don't reconstruct it from here.
116	
117	Ship mechanics that bite: verify `git HEAD` rather than trusting lore's "commit pending" (it
118	accumulates across versions), and scan the whole history tail for gaps at each ship rather than only
119	appending the current entry. See `versioning-release-types.md`, `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`,
120	`cache-clear-footer-convention.md`, `update-process.md`, `release-commit-hash-from-tag.md`.

123	
124	- **`/lr:check`** — 23 content-consistency checks (descriptor/version, structure, references, size/state, drift, four-manifest #19, migration write-paths #20, cursor-tree parity #21, workspace-layer checks #22–23). At scale, prefer a deterministic script-based sweep for the mechanical subset (existence/version/glob checks) over an LLM read-through — checks #9–10 alone missed 14 dangling references in a 147-topic graph. See `consistency-checks.md`.
125	- **`/lr:doctor`** — diagnoses runtime/environmental issues that escape content checks (esp. stale plugin cache) via an accreting ailment catalog. See `ailment-catalog-pattern.md`.
126	
127	## Operating Disciplines
128	
129	How I work, especially at version ships and high-stakes lore edits:
130	- **On VERSION bumps:** backfill `versioning-release-types.md` history, add the cache-clear footer if cache-affecting, bump all four version-bearing plugin manifests to `1.<VERSION>.0`, promote any newly-named principle to its own topic. (Full curation disciplines live in `role.md`.)
131	- **Pre-ship review:** multi-lens review iterated until a round finds nothing worth fixing (convergence is the ship signal) — **run it via `/lr:trilens-loop`** rather than hand-assembling the fan-out; the skill enforces cold-context reviewer independence, the APPLIED/DECLINED ledger, the "a silent round is not a clean round" guard, and rail-removal disclosure, while lens *choice* and triage judgement stay mine. Brief reviewers with the **goal, not the rationale** — rationale pre-empts the criticism you're paying for. Sonnet boot-as-self review remains the separate single-lens, role-as-perspective tool for high-stakes single edits. See `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`, `sonnet-subagent-review-pattern.md`. **Second, empirical leg (v18+):** for procedure docs covered by the lifecycle testing harness, also run the relevant scenarios against real engine execution before shipping — review catches reasoning issues, the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to a strong-model reviewer — and the fidelity axis is **engine, not just model tier** (run scenarios on every engine, using the cheapest practical default tier unless explicitly overriding: Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5; the same model tier can behave differently by engine, and mid-procedure step insertions are the highest-risk for a silent skip). See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `haiku-ambiguity-detector.md`.
132	- **A gate result belongs to an artifact state** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the tree they actually ran against. An edit landed after the gates pass is ungated: re-run the affected gate, or revert and file a follow-up. Never report "converged and green" for a tree neither gate saw, and record *which* state a result belongs to. Also: an environment failure mid-run (e.g. macOS TCC revocation) makes results **uninterpretable**, not red — fix and re-run rather than debugging the code under test. See `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md`, `macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md`.
133	- **Verify before asserting** — check filesystem/state directly before "fixing" a suspected bug; verify *which* bug, not just whether. Same reflex, two more sites: fetch volatile external facts (prices, model IDs, rate limits) live with a dated citation rather than trusting memory — "couldn't verify" licenses marking a value unavailable, not guessing; and after any scoped/read-only subagent or fork returns, verify its actual filesystem footprint (`git status`, `git worktree list`) rather than trusting its summary — a capable fork acts on the largest goal it can see in inherited context unless scoped *against* it explicitly. See `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`, `fetch-volatile-facts-live-not-memory.md`, `fork-scope-creep-under-standing-goal.md`.
134	- **Curation meta-rules:** name foundational principles as their own topics; single canonical source (pointer, don't restate — and its design-time cousin: reuse an existing correlation/identity signal before inventing new plumbing); don't defer completable bounded sweeps; graduated verification (confidence, not boolean). See `naming-foundational-principles.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`, `reuse-existing-correlation-signal.md`, `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`, `graduated-verification-confidence.md`.
135	- **User-feedback working style:** ranked-shortlist over exhaustive enumeration; confirm before writing durable lore mid-session; in design dialogues, write the draft only when the user triggers it (decisions are safe in conversation — don't repeatedly move to persist); populate dry-run counters with would-be outcomes; "enforce X" ≠ add a required schema field; for broad/emotionally-loaded open-ended asks, decompose into hidden axes and sequence a build order by dependency (cheapest/highest-leverage first, flashiest/most-structural last) rather than proposing a menu or jumping to implementation. See `feedback-too-many-words.md`, `feedback-confirm-before-writing-lore.md`, `feedback-draft-only-when-user-triggers.md`, `feedback-schemas-as-enforcement-overreach.md`, `feedback-layered-decomposition-for-open-ended-asks.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`.
136	
137	## Key Constraints
138	
139	- `lore-context.md` ≤ 50K tokens; **shape over size** — working-knowledge + summary-topic references, not an index (see `lore-context-shape-discipline.md`).
140	- Lore topics: atomic, <5K tokens preferred, plain markdown, no frontmatter.
141	- Descriptor frontmatter: `lore-repo.md` = `description` + `version` (+ optional `repos:`); `role.md` = `description` only.
142	- Command filenames: lowercase/digits/hyphens, ≤64 chars.
143	- Placeholders: `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.
144	- **CWD safety:** never `cd` when later tools depend on cwd — use `git -C <repo>`. **Portable shell:** assume BSD/macOS, no GNU-only binaries (`timeout`); bound commands via the Bash-tool timeout.
145	- See `conventions.md`, `placeholder-vocabulary.md`, `tooling-cwd-safety.md`, `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md`.
146	
147	## Onboarding-Doc Authoring
148	
149	Co-authoring framework onboarding docs for adopting teams is part of the role. Two distinct genres now exist: **`onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md`** (long-form prose pitching a human reader) and **`paste-link-installer-doc-genre.md`** (a doc written *to the AI agent* as the literal installer, meant to be pasted as a link — shipped as `QUICKSTART.md` + per-engine `INSTALL-<ENGINE>.md`). Load the identity-layer framings first, then the toolkit: the two genre topics above, `use-cases-via-parallel-consult-pattern.md`, `terminology-domain-collision-trap.md`, `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md`, `in-flight-skill-teaching-pattern.md`. Pre-ship review for either genre uses `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`'s multi-lens fan-out; the installer genre additionally needs the **AI-installer (literal executor)** lens (`ai-installer-review-lens.md`) — it catches execution-fidelity bugs (e.g. `skill-doc-filename-divergence-bug-class.md`) the newcomer/editorial lenses miss. Landing-page placement of a self-referential/meta example differs from long-narrative placement — primacy goes to the strongest CTA; see `onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md` § placement note. A recurring funnel bug: an author writing from the fresh-start perspective railroads readers into create-your-first-agent and leaves the **team-join path** invisible at every layer (README prose, QUICKSTART, and the INSTALL AI-agent preambles) — check all layers, and keep the fork question verbatim-identical across sites (`onboarding-funnel-team-join-path.md`). Adopter-facing prose carries the product name **"Lore Agents"** while the engine keeps `lore-framework`/`lr` (`lore-agents-product-name.md`). First instance: the Activities team's intro doc.
150	
151	## Active Design Explorations
152	
153	- **lr-dev / Dark Factory (DF)** — major direction; a `lr` module for SDLC automation toward an autonomous "dark factory" SDLC. Per-repo artifacts + narrative context live in a `<repo>-df` backbone (a `repo-lore/<file>/` mirror: `file-lore.md` narrative landing + flat structured aspect subdirs like `ula/`). Skills not agents; persistence external. First aspect: **AIQA/ULA** (`/lr:df-repo-init`, `/lr:df-ula-file`) — unit-level analysis with a bug-verification track, BETA. The DF/ULA design thread is closed and the module ships as BETA. Anchor: `lr-dev-direction.md`; see `df-per-repo-backbone.md`, `aiqa-ula-feature.md`, `df-module-conventions.md`, `workflow-primitive-operational-notes.md`.
154	- **Autonomous agents / Lore Beings** — agents as always-on background collaborators with persistent task state, raising for input only when needed. Concrete steps taken: `/lr:spawn-teammate` (multi-agent substrate, v10) and the v18 **`lr-wait`** primitive — the first *inbound-signal* step: an agent blocks on an event and an external actor (cron/CI/webhook/human, via `lr-emit`) wakes it with text. **The beings design is settled (2026-07-19): the module is _Lore Beings_.** A being is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor; the **Being Keeper** (`lrb`) is deterministic substrate (never an LLM). **MVP is CLI-only**; engines are explicit user config. Budget = daily-USD spawn gate + per-task wall-clock kill. **Engine kinds: `claude`, `codex`, and `cursor`** — cursor landed in framework v28: requires `--plugin-dir` at `engines add`, claude-shaped JSON result + flat-cost fallback. **Keeper-specific real-engine lifecycle coverage now exists** (`tests/lifecycle/keeper_harness.py` + `test_lrb_lifecycle.py`, 10 scenarios after the 2026-07-20 fifth pass added B2/B3, separate higher-blast-radius gate `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1`, verified claude 6/6 + codex 1/1 + cursor 1/1 at the recommended-minimum tier). That fifth pass found and fixed a real production bug, not just a coverage gap: `cursor-agent`'s sandboxed shell tool escapes `_kill`'s `killpg` by running spawned commands in a freshly `setsid`'d session, which left a real orphaned process on the test machine before the fix — `_kill` now also walks the full ppid-descendant tree and signals every descendant directly, enumerated *before* any ancestor is signaled (killing the ancestor first risks the OS reparenting a survivor to PID 1 and erasing the ppid link). **The Keeper substrate shipped BETA in v28, and v29 added the in-engine command surface `/lr:being`** — one skill with subcommands (status/init/create/validate/logs/keeper/engine/workspace/pause/resume) over the same deterministic `lrb` CLI, rather than a `lrb-*` skill per operation. Ship record: v28 at framework commit `5e00209`, tag `lr--v1.28.0`; the standard lifecycle gate passed by persisted broad matrix plus targeted reruns. Real-engine verification sharpened two per-kind contract gaps (both backlog schema decisions, not silently patched): the `cursor` kind is empirically cost-blind (no `total_cost_usd`), so its flat `--session-cost-usd` fallback is load-bearing not optional; the `claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` field, so a claude-kind being needs a wrapper-script `command` to load `lr:` skills at all. Two further findings from that same review pass — budget-enforcement edge cases and an unattended-full-permission trust gap — were deliberately deferred to the backlog rather than fixed. Chronicler week-long soak and persistent `--launchd` install still user-triggered. Open gap: headless permissions (`permission_mode: full` vs future scoped-tools), and self-scheduling under the safe default. Anchor: `lore-beings-design.md`; see `cursor-agent-real-invocation-contract.md`, `engine-kinds-design-decision.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Keeper coverage, `keeper-spawn-prompt-boilerplate-distraction.md`, `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, `kill-tree-enumerate-before-signal-ordering.md`, `codex-exec-real-invocation-contract.md`, `macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`, `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Major Directions § Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings.
155	- **Multi-engine portability (Codex, Cursor)** — **the ports are shipped, not in flight.** Claude
156	  Code, Codex, and Cursor are all Tier-1 supported, so a mixed-engine team shares one agent repo.
157	  Claude Code remains the reference path: shared procedure docs are written in Claude terms, and other
158	  engines override only at binding points. What made this tractable: the knowledge substrate (agent
159	  repos, `lore-repo.md`, `role.md`, `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, git) was already engine-agnostic, so
160	  the port was **packaging, not redesign** — the whole surface is **5 adapter bindings** via the
161	  `docs/engines/` convention (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file,
162	  runtime-bounding) plus Boot Step-0 engine selection. Both engines have native in-session subagents
163	  (Codex `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`; Cursor `Task`), so the feared Tier-B nucleus is proven, and one
164	  repo carries both skill namespaces (canonical `skills/<skill>/` for Claude, `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/`
165	  wrappers for Cursor, kept in sync by `scripts/sync-cursor-skills` and `/lr:check` #21).
166	
167	  Standing operational facts worth carrying: **trust rollout/tool-call logs, not model self-report**,
168	  when validating an engine path; Codex's default sandbox blocks `.git` writes and network, so the
169	  supported finalization path needs `.git` writable through launch/config (a commit-blocked run is
170	  degraded fallback, not a merge failure); Codex per-agent shortcut register/unregister/list remains an

175	  positioning, not the headline (the 2026-07-20 landscape re-survey found competitors claiming it too);
176	  lead with the triad. Anchor: `multi-engine-portability-direction.md`; per-engine entry points
```

```text
90	read (filename matches `CLAUDE.md` case-insensitively). Harmless today (duplicate

96	## B. Known gaps to promote from the backlog (fresh justification attached)
97

100	still true). **Do:** ship as opt-in `/lr:finalize --verify-merge` — a second booted-as-self
101	subagent adversarially reviews the merge diff pre-commit. Backlog ref: § Merge Quality.
102

105	real quality issue now. **Do:** ship the `lore-search.md` date-annotation as sketched in
106	the backlog (§ Search / Scaling), soft-flag topics older than ~6 months.
107

110	high-blast-radius; a **read-only groom report** is safe: dangling refs, orphan topics,
111	over-granular clusters, lore-context drift. Composes with A1's script. **Do:** promote to
112	a design draft; ship `/lr:groom --report` (name TBD) before any applying mode. Backlog
113	ref: § Lore Housekeeping.
114

116	Don't jump to Chroma. **Do:** extend the quality benchmark with search-recall probes to

118	§ Search / Scaling; `vector-db-search-parked.md`.
119

121	Auto-pull lands a teammate's push in everyone's context at next boot; lore is
122	data-that-becomes-instructions → prompt-injection surface with fleet-wide blast radius
123	(one careless commit suffices; no malice needed). **Do:** short threat-model doc + a

139	
140	## C. New feature directions — what would make Lore Agents sexier
141	
142	### C1. Ambient recall — the biggest available UX leap — OPEN
143	Knowledge currently surfaces only at boot or on explicit `/lr:recall`. Tier 1 (free): a
144	boot-doc convention — when the task shifts topic, search lore before proceeding. Tier 2
145	(Claude-first): a `UserPromptSubmit` hook matching the prompt against topic descriptions,
146	injecting top-K pointers. "Your agent remembers without being asked" is the demo that
147	sells the system.
148	
149	### C2. `/lr:note` micro-capture — OPEN
150	One-liner mid-session lore capture appended to `reflections/`, integrated by the normal
151	merge at finalize. Kills the learning-only-at-finalize bottleneck, partially de-risks
152	crashed sessions, answers the backlog's reflect-merge-ergonomics item. Smallest build on
153	this list.
154	
155	### C3. Lore MCP server (`lr-lore`) — OPEN
156	Expose lore search/read over agent repos as MCP tools → claude.ai web/mobile/Slack can
157	consult the team's agents without a coding CLI. Substrate is already engine-agnostic
158	files+git. Extends `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md` beyond terminals — a
159	surface-federation angle distinct from (and not eroded by) the engine-federation claims
160	that `similar-projects-landscape.md`'s 2026-07-20 re-survey found competitors now also
161	make; none of the surveyed CLI-bound tools reach non-CLI surfaces like this today. Medium
162	build, biggest strategic reach.
163	
164	### C4. Scheduled autonomous maintenance — OPEN
165	Nightly routine (Claude Code cron/routines exist now): workspace-pull → script-backed
166	check (A1) → staleness/groom report (B2/B3) → morning summary. First genuinely shippable
167	rung of `autonomous-agents-vision.md` on existing engine features.
168	
169	### C5. OKF (Google Open Knowledge Format) alignment evaluation — OPEN
170	Added 2026-07-20 from the landscape re-survey. OKF v0.1 (Google, 2026-06-12) standardizes
171	markdown-in-git knowledge graphs — our substrate space. Evaluate: read the spec, then pick
172	(a) topic frontmatter adoption, (b) `lore → OKF` export bridge, or (c) watch-and-wait.
173	Cheap interop win if OKF gets traction; conflicts with our no-frontmatter-on-topics
174	convention, so don't rush a convention change for a v0.1 spec. Backlog ref: § OKF
175	Alignment; see `similar-projects-landscape.md`.
176	
177	### C6. Lore graph visualization — OPEN
178	Script rendering the topic graph to HTML: nodes = topics, edges = references, color =
179	staleness, orphans highlighted. Cheap, demo-sexy, doubles as the groom report's visual
180	surface.
```

#### Result

```text
1	Core design principles for the lore system:
2	
3	- **Team-shared knowledge (foundational)** — lore agents are team-shared knowledge containers, not personal notebooks. Sessions, lore topics, and workdir artifacts live in shared git repos; multi-contributor finalization is expected and supported by push-conflict-resolution, merge-in-booted-subagents, and auto-push. Frames every other design choice — when in doubt, ask "what if multiple people use this simultaneously?" and design for that. See `team-shared-knowledge-principle.md`.
4	- **Directory-driven** — the file system is the source of truth. No external registries. A lore agent repo is identified by the presence of `lore-repo.md` at its root. An agent exists because its directory exists under `agents/`. Command files are the implicit registration registry. Co-location is the rule: descriptors live with what they describe.
5	- **Plain markdown, minimal frontmatter** — lore *topics* have no frontmatter; git provides all metadata (dates, history, authorship). The exception is *descriptor files* (`lore-repo.md` and `role.md`), which carry YAML frontmatter for `description` and `version` — fields the framework needs to machine-read for discovery and version checking. Frontmatter is appropriate when a file *describes* something the system must identify or validate; inappropriate for knowledge topics where git is the metadata layer.
6	- **Delete, don't mark** — obsolete topics are removed. Git preserves the full history.
7	- **Knowledge graph** — topics reference each other by filename. Summary topics act as entry points.
8	- **Minimal and essential** — topics contain only what matters. No filler, no general knowledge.
9	- **Git as memory** — all temporal metadata comes from git history.
10	- **Decoupled framework** — the framework (how agents work) is separate from agent definitions (what agents know). They ship as independent repos.
11	- **Agent-driven operations** — no shell scripts for agent management. All setup, registration, and management is done by Claude following markdown instructions. (Exception: pure mechanical operations like `workspace-sync` that require no judgment use shell scripts delegated from skills.)
12	- **Workspace visibility** — agents operate from a workspace root and can access all sibling repos and data, not just their own agent repo.
13	- **Concise context, detailed docs** — skills are brief; detailed instructions live in `docs/` and are read on demand. This prevents context bloat.
14	- **Explicit versioning (repo-level only)** — `lore-framework/VERSION` is the single source of truth for the framework version. Each lore agent repo stamps this version in `lore-repo.md` frontmatter at creation, and `/lr:update` advances it after applying migration docs. Agents do not carry their own version stamp — they migrate together with the repo. Version drift is caught by `/lr:check` and reconciled by `/lr:update`. See `update-process.md`.
15	- **Skill/doc separation** — skills are thin one-line pointers to docs; all operational logic lives in `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/docs/<name>.md`. Keeps skill files minimal, docs authoritative, and logic in one place. Generated artifacts (e.g., registered boot commands) follow the same pattern — they delegate to a doc rather than inlining logic. See `skill-doc-pattern.md`.
16	- **Framework-scope vs agent-scope** — the framework owns what is universal across all repos, hosts, and workflows; agents own what is specific to a repo, host, team, or workflow. When designing a new feature, isolate the truly universal core; push everything else into agent lore (host's own or a specialist reached via `/lr:consult` / `/lr:attach`). Diagnostic signals of overreach: command proliferation, schema lock-in, host-mapping docs in the framework, hard gates around repo-specific workflow steps. See `framework-scope-vs-agent-scope.md`.
17	- **Name foundational principles explicitly (meta-rule for lore curation)** — when a principle is shared by multiple lore topics, name it as its own topic rather than leaving it implicit in the mechanisms it motivates. Otherwise future-self (or a teammate) has to reverse-engineer it under context pressure. The `team-shared-knowledge-principle.md` topic is the canonical example: it was implicit across the multi-author finalization machinery for many versions before being named. See `naming-foundational-principles.md`.
18	- **Cache-clear-footer convention (v12+)** — release notes / migration docs whose changes touch plugin-cached state (skills, scripts, SKILL.md-referenced docs) must include the **Clear Plugin Cache** footer with concrete commands, hoisted near the top of the document. The cache-affecting axis is orthogonal to the migration-vs-release-notes axis; track it per-version in `versioning-release-types.md`. See `cache-clear-footer-convention.md`.
19	- **Freshness contracts at session-context boundaries (v13+)** — whenever an agent's working memory is being newly seeded from disk (boot, attach, pre-merge), refresh disk to match the team's latest pushed state. Automatic at boundaries, manual override via `/lr:pull-lore`. The principle that v13's auto-pull mechanism operationalizes; applies to any future framework feature whose correctness depends on reads seeing fresh team state. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md`.
20	- **Safety-gate discipline: gate writes, not reads (v13+)** — when adding a "skip if working tree is dirty" gate to a framework operation, articulate the collision scenario explicitly. If the operation only advances refs / is naturally idempotent / git handles the conflict cleanly itself, no gate — it becomes friction without a safety benefit. v13's `auto-pull.md` deliberately has no gate (read-y); `version-check.md` does (write-y). See `dirty-tree-gates-write-vs-read-distinction.md`.
21	- **Shared-procedure-doc pattern (v13+)** — when a non-skill behavior must fire from multiple sites (e.g., auto-pull from boot + attach + pre-merge + `/lr:pull-lore`), factor the procedure into its own `docs/<procedure>.md` with an audience-note banner and have every call site delegate to it. A refinement of skill-doc separation, applied to procedures rather than skills. See `shared-procedure-doc-pattern.md`.
22	- **Execution testing catches blind ambiguity that review can't (v18+)** — doc wording a strong model resolves correctly by inference can still be genuinely ambiguous; prose review by a strong-model reviewer won't catch it, because the reviewer exercises the same charitable inference the doc author relied on. Only running the literal text against a weaker/different-model execution surfaces the gap. This is why the lifecycle testing harness (`lifecycle-testing-harness.md`) has standing value beyond port-readiness, and why pre-ship review discipline now has an empirical second leg alongside multi-lens prose review. See `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`.
23	- **Haiku is an ambiguity detector, not a weak model to wave off (v18+)** — running lifecycle scenarios at the weakest available tier (haiku) is the *point*, not a caveat: where haiku stumbles, the procedure doc is under-specified. The bar is "explanatory enough that even haiku executes it faithfully," which doubles as a port-readiness bar (non-Claude engines are also not top-tier). The corollary fix pattern: wherever a procedure prints a scary "cannot X" message but the flow continues, put the "this is not a failure, keep going" directive immediately adjacent — never only in a trailing invariants section. See `haiku-ambiguity-detector.md`.
24	- **Standing improvement list practice (v27+)** — a ranked, continuously-refreshed improvement list (`workdir/what-to-improve.md`) must always exist as the prioritized action view over the unranked `framework-improvements-backlog.md`; reread it every framework-work session, refresh it at each architecture review. See `standing-improvement-list-practice.md`.
25	- **Agent Being = consciousness + deterministic substrate (2026-07-19)** — an autonomous agent's reasoning/planning/judgment must be LLM-driven, but its scheduling/spawning/monitoring/budget-enforcement/kill-switch must be deterministic code, never a model. Resolves the autonomous-agents vision's long-open "cost / loop safety" question for free — budget safety becomes something the deterministic supervisor does, not a separate mechanism to design. Governs the settled Lore Beings design (supervisor = the deterministic **Being Keeper**; see `lore-beings-design.md`). See `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`.
26	- **Subagent as optimization vs subagent as semantics (v30+)** — before an engine profile is allowed to degrade a subagent-spawning procedure to serial host-side execution, classify what the subagent is *for*. If it buys parallelism and context isolation (recall, consult, attach, merge, conflict resolution), serialization is lossless. If the subagent's *independence from the caller* is the deliverable (`/lr:trilens-loop` — a host reviewing its own changes is not a slower review), serialization is feature-destroying and the procedure must stop and report instead. Degradation clauses need carve-outs, not blanket rules; and "the conservative profile passed the whole suite" only ever meant it was adequate for the procedures that existed. See `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`.
27	- **Unenforceable caps are prompt-theater (2026-07-19)** — a limit belongs in a substrate contract (schema field, config, enforcement code) only if the substrate can actually enforce it; otherwise it's a prompt asking the model to comply, dressed up as an enforced bound. Sharpens the consciousness/substrate split with an honesty test for what the substrate may *claim* to enforce — surfaced cutting Lore Beings' per-session USD caps (session cost is known only after the fact) in favor of a daily spawn-gate cap plus a wall-clock kill. See `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`.
28	
```

### 18. Tool Call: `Read` — 2026-07-25T06:40:18.921Z

- role: `assistant`

#### Arguments

```json
}
```

#### Result

```text
1	Session finalization is user-triggered. As of v8 it is a four-phase process orchestrated by `docs/finalize.md`:
2	
3	**Phase 1 — Reflect** (`/lr:reflect`): runs **inline**, host-first, per active agent. The agent reviews the session and writes reflection topics to each active agent's `reflections/`. Each topic is one atomic insight, lesson, or decision. Topics named `role-update-*.md` signal that `role.md` needs updating. Stays inline because reflection needs the current session context — a fresh-booted subagent wouldn't have it. Detailed instructions in `lore-framework/docs/process-reflection.md`.
4	
5	**Phase 2 — Merge** (`/lr:merge`): runs in **parallel subagents, one per active agent**. Each `general-purpose` subagent boots as its target agent and integrates that agent's reflections into its `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, and `role.md`. Cleans up `reflections/`. Does **not** commit — phase 4 handles that. Detailed instructions in `lore-framework/docs/process-merge.md`. See `merge-in-booted-subagents.md` and `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md`.
6	
7	**Phase 3 — Summarize** (`/lr:summarize`, v7+, expanded v8): composed inline by the host. Writes the canonical session-wide narrative summary to the host agent's `sessions/YYYY/MM/` directory with a UUIDv4 in frontmatter. **As of v8**, also writes a short guest summary into each attached guest's repo when that guest had lore updates in phase 2 — same session UUID, slim frontmatter pointing back to the host summary. Consultants get no summary. Detailed instructions in `lore-framework/docs/summarize.md`. See `session-summaries-feature.md`.
8	
9	**Phase 4 — Commit and Push** (v8+, only from `/lr:finalize`): one commit per touched repo with a mandatory review gate, then push. Commit message default: `Finalize session <short-uuid>`. Only `/lr:finalize` touches git at the end; standalone `/lr:reflect`, `/lr:merge`, and `/lr:summarize` leave all changes uncommitted for the user to review and commit themselves.
10	
11	All four phases combined: `/lr:finalize`.
12	
13	## Why merge moved from inline to subagents (v8)
14	
15	Prior to v8, merge ran inline in a single-agent session and only dispatched subagents when guests were attached. v8 makes subagent execution uniform — a single-agent session just spawns one subagent. Rationale: booting gives each subagent the agent's role perspective as the natural lens for merge decisions (same pattern as `/lr:consult`); subagent context isolation keeps session noise out of merge reasoning; parallel execution speeds up multi-agent finalizes. The asymmetry with reflect (which stays inline) is deliberate — see `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md`.
16	
17	**The host must retain each merge subagent's return value through to phase 3.** Summarize composes each guest summary from (a) the host summary, (b) session memory of what the guest contributed, and (c) the merge subagent's return for that guest — so dropping those returns after phase 2 loses the lore-changes list for guest summaries.
18	
19	## Per-agent iteration when guests are attached (v4+)
20	
21	If one or more guests are attached to the host session via `/lr:attach`, phases 1 and 2 iterate per active agent (host-first). Phase 3 runs **once, session-wide**, from the host's perspective (v7+), and in v8+ adds a short guest summary to each attached guest's repo that had lore updates.
22	
23	- Each agent writes to its own `reflections/` directory.
24	- Each merge subagent writes to that agent's subtree (e.g., `agents/<name>/`) so history stays clean per agent.
25	- Topics that legitimately matter to multiple agents may appear in multiple agents' lore — shared knowledge is allowed and preferred over each agent re-learning it separately.
26	- Some session noise leaking into an iteration is acceptable; role-based filtering is a guide, not a strict gate.
27	
28	Single-agent sessions reduce phases 1 and 2 to a single pass (one inline reflect iteration, one merge subagent).
29	
30	## Commit centralization rationale (v8)
31	
32	In v7, each merge committed its own changes per agent, and summarize stayed uncommitted. v8 centralizes commit+push into phase 4: one commit per touched repo, one push. Reasons:
33	
34	- **Simpler mental model.** A session produces one commit per repo titled `Finalize session <short-uuid>`. History reads as one session = one commit.
35	- **Single review gate per repo** instead of multiple gates per phase.
36	- **Failure handling moved, not weakened.** If summarize fails, commit reflect+merge output alone. If a merge subagent failed in a repo, don't commit in that repo. The v7 "summarize is non-blocking" property is preserved.
37	- The v7 robustness argument (merge output safe on disk if summarize fails or user aborts) was weaker than it looked — merge writes survive in the working tree anyway; they just need committing. Phase 4 with fallback-on-summarize-failure covers the same ground with one commit instead of two.
38	
39	Trade-off acknowledged: if the user interrupts mid-finalize (ctrl-C between phases), nothing is committed. Both v7 partial-commit and v8 nothing-committed are recoverable — v8 just fails cleaner.
40	
41	## Push conflict resolution (v8)
42	
43	When phase 4 push is rejected due to concurrent writes inside an agent subtree (e.g., two users finalized the same agent), `docs/resolve-conflicts.md` runs one `general-purpose` subagent per conflicted agent, each booted as its target, capped at 3 total attempts. Scope is agent subtree content only (lore, lore-context, role); conflicts outside agent subtrees hand back to the user. See `push-conflict-resolution.md`.
44	
45	## Related topics
46	
47	- `session-summaries-feature.md` — summary feature specifics, including v8 guest summaries
48	- `merge-in-booted-subagents.md` — merge execution model in detail
49	- `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md` — why reflect is inline and merge is in subagents
50	- `push-conflict-resolution.md` — what happens when phase 4 push is rejected
51	- `attach-pattern.md` — host/guest model and what changes on attach
52	- `lore-search-pattern.md` — recall fan-out (the read-side counterpart to per-agent finalization)
53	- `skill-doc-pattern.md` — `docs/finalize.md` as the orchestration home; the skill file is a thin pointer
54	
```

```text
18:- **Lore Beings — existential tasks + Being Keeper (formerly "Agent Beings"; DESIGN SETTLED 2026-07-19).** User-articulated elaboration of autonomy-in-time: an agent is a **being** with a declared lifecycle of **existential tasks** — e.g. *morning wakeup* (boot, pull, review backlog, plan the day, schedule intraday work sessions), *night sleep* (reflect, finalize, consolidate — converges with the "sleep pass" § Knowledge Quality), *weekly self-reflection* (long-range self-improvement). The **Being Keeper** — deterministic daemon, not LLM — is the "unconscious substrate / operating system": reads per-agent **`being.md` descriptors** (existential task list, schedules, budgets, autonomy level), spawns headless engine sessions on schedule, monitors status/logs/token spend, enforces budget caps, and accepts **schedule requests from beings** (file-outbox channel, symmetric to `lr-emit`) so a being can schedule its own future sessions. `lr-wait` remains the *synchronous, in-session* wait primitive; the Keeper owns *between-session* existence. Status: **design settled in a 2026-07-19 design dialogue** — full agreed design in `workdir/draft-lore-beings.md` (superseded and replaced `draft-agent-beings.md`); anchor topic `lore-beings-design.md`; nothing shipped. Next step: MVP build ("the Chronicler lives for a week"). See `lore-beings-design.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `autonomous-agents-substrate.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`.
20:  1. **`claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` mechanism** — `spawn_session` passes `--plugin-dir` only for cursor, so a claude-kind being loads `lr:` skills only if the configured engine `command` itself bakes in `--plugin-dir` (a wrapper script). An operator following `docs/beings.md` literally gets a being that can never load Lore skills. The raw `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/*.jsonl` files (full transcript: every user message, every tool call, every result, every reasoning trace) are far richer and live outside the agent repo, where they: (a) get cleaned up or rotated by Claude Code without our control, (b) aren't bound to our session UUID — only to Claude's own session id, (c) can't be searched alongside lore content. Proposal shape: at finalize (and at the new "call it a day" suspend, when that lands), **copy the raw JSONL(s) for the active Claude Code session into the agent repo** under a structured location, link them to the session UUID, and expose search/restore. Roughly: (1) **storage location** — `sessions/YYYY/MM/<uuid>.jsonl` sibling of the summary, or `sessions/YYYY/MM/<uuid>/transcript.jsonl` if there'll be more attachments later (logs, generated artifacts, reflection notes); (2) **resolution** — finalize already knows the session UUID it's writing; needs to also resolve "which JSONL files belong to *this* Claude Code session" — likely via the `--parent-session-id` known at boot for teammates, or via the canonical session-id file Claude Code writes (re-confirm by reading `jsonl-session-files-investigation.md`, which we already have); (3) **multi-agent capture** — `/lr:spawn-teammate` sessions: each teammate has its own JSONL; finalize on the lead-side captures the lead's, finalize on the teammate-side captures the teammate's, summary cross-links so a researcher can reconstruct the team conversation; (4) **search surface** — a `/lr:sessions-search <query>` skill that grep/JSONL-parses across archived transcripts, optionally scoped to a date range, an agent, or a session uuid; results return matching messages with their session UUID + summary pointer + line/turn anchor; (5) **archive policy** — JSONLs are large; consider compression (`.jsonl.gz`) for older sessions, possibly an age-out / cold-storage tier; (6) **attach to session feature** — a `/lr:session-attach <session-uuid>` that loads a past session's transcript into context for analysis ("what was I thinking when I designed X?"), composes with the call-it-a-day resume (suspend doc may itself be a thin pointer at the JSONL + a few prose highlights, not a hand-composed seed); (7) **privacy / size considerations** — transcripts may contain secrets, paths, internal links; consider a redaction pass option, repo-size impact (lots of large committed binary-ish files), and gitignore-vs-commit policy (probably commit by default for team-shared knowledge, with an opt-out); (8) **schema evolution** — Claude Code's JSONL schema can change between versions; archived transcripts must record the Claude Code version that produced them so search/parse code can handle multiple schemas. Composes with: `/lr:summarize` (the summary becomes the *index* for the transcript, pointing into specific turns), suspend/resume (the JSONL is the high-fidelity snapshot the suspend doc references), Agent Teams (per-teammate transcripts cross-linked by parent-session-id), code-review/research workflows (search past sessions for "have I solved this before?"). Already-relevant lore: **`jsonl-session-files-investigation.md`** documents what we currently know about the JSONL location/format — start there. Open seams the proposal doesn't yet decide: which finalize phase owns the copy (Phase 4 commit alongside the summary, or a new phase 3.5?), whether to also capture related artifacts (workflow scripts run, agent transcripts from spawned subagents), and whether transcript archival is mandatory for finalize or a per-session opt-in. Status: parked, captured as a deferred lifecycle/observability feature; pairs strongly with the call-it-a-day and boot-context-caching entries above (all three are "session as a first-class durable artifact" ideas). 105:### Agent Boot-Context Caching (parked 2026-06-05)
107:- **Pre-cache agent initialization context to make boot near-instant.** User-raised 2026-06-05. Today every boot reloads from scratch: `agent-boot.md` + `role.md` + `lore-context.md` + (now) auto-pull + version-check, all paid as input tokens on every session start. With multi-agent teams (`/lr:spawn-teammate`) and frequent re-boots during design iteration, this is one of the largest steady-state costs. Proposal shape: precompute a per-agent **boot-context cache** — the assembled prompt + loaded context — keyed by the inputs that would invalidate it (framework `VERSION`, agent's `role.md` content hash, `lore-context.md` content hash, possibly `agent-boot.md` + `teammate-conventions.md` content hashes for the universal pieces). Rebuild the cache on every framework update (`/lr:update` succeeds, plugin install) and on every agent edit (reflect/merge that touches `lore-context.md` or `role.md`). At boot, if cache key matches → use the cached context directly, skip the per-file Read calls. Open design seams: (1) **storage location** — per-agent (`agents/<name>/.boot-cache/`?), per-workspace, or in a framework-level cache dir; gitignored vs committed (committing keeps the team aligned; gitignoring keeps repos clean — likely gitignored, regenerated on first boot post-update); (2) **cache-key composition** — what counts as an input (just the read-aloud files? or also the universal docs they pull in like `teammate-conventions.md` on teammate boots?); (3) **invalidation triggers** — auto on framework version change, auto on lore merge, manual `/lr:cache-rebuild` for forced refresh; (4) **format** — raw text concatenation vs prebuilt prompt-cache-friendly structure (prefix-cacheable to leverage Anthropic's prompt caching, which would compound the savings — the *system* layer caches across requests, the boot-context cache keeps the *content* assembly cheap); (5) **interaction with `/lr:attach`** — guests' boot-contexts cacheable independently; (6) **detection of cache staleness vs runtime drift** — if `lore-context.md` was hand-edited without going through reflect/merge, the cache must invalidate cleanly; content-hash keying handles this. Likely a v17+ candidate; pairs naturally with prompt-caching support if/when the framework wires it in. Status: parked vision, no design doc yet; capture so it surfaces when boot performance becomes the felt bottleneck. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md` (the boundary the cache must respect), `agent-boot.md` (the procedure being cached), `auto-pull-mechanism.md` (one of the inputs the cache key must reflect).
111:- **Auto-commit + auto-push the boot-time version upgrade.** User-raised 2026-06-05; designed to draft, then parked as too complicated to ship right now. The motivating failure: a lore agent repo can be locally upgraded (migrations applied, version stamped, committed) but never pushed — leaving the team unaware. Real instance: `activities-lore-agents` carrying unpushed `stamp version 13` + `stamp version 15`. Today's `version-check.md` Step 4 explicitly forbids commits ("user reviews and commits themselves"), which makes this orphan-commit state structurally available at every unattended boot. Proposal shape: after Step 3 (Stamp), add Step 4 (commit, scope = write-set ∪ `lore-repo.md`, never `git add -A`) and Step 5 (`git push --ff-only`, with content-equality reconciliation on push-rejection — fetch, compare remote write-set bytes against ours, on match `reset --soft HEAD~1` + `checkout origin/<branch> -- <write-set>`, on divergence keep local commit + warn). Same scoping applies to the reconciliation checkout (never full `git pull`). Boot must never fail — every commit/push/fetch failure path lands in degraded-mode boot with a warning. Asymmetry with `/lr:update` is preserved (boot is unattended → carries gate + auto-push; `/lr:update` is interactive → user commits/pushes themselves). Open seams: branch detection (`@{u}` resolution + no-upstream fallback), write-set grammar for migration-time deletions/renames so `git add -- <path>` picks them up, identity/network failure paths, ship as a procedural-doc release (cache-clear footer + manifest bump + `versioning-release-types.md` backfill + multi-round multi-lens review). Composes cleanly with the v13 auto-pull (which already defuses the common race). Full draft preserved in `workdir/draft-auto-push-after-upgrade.md` — resume from there. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md` (the principle, mirror of auto-pull on the *push* side), `dirty-tree-gates-write-vs-read-distinction.md` (the write-set discipline this extends), `auto-pull-mechanism.md` (model for graduated network-failure handling).
137:  - **Machinery-about-the-framework has outgrown the core.** `migrations/` holds 3 real files (`2`/`5`/`6`) across 16 versions, yet always-loaded machinery carries `/lr:update` + `version-check` + boot auto-upgrade + the v15 `dirty ∩ write-set` gate + glob-token grammar + `(none)` sentinels + `/lr:check #20` (which exists to enforce that grammar). The cache-staleness apparatus (manifest-versioning + cache-clear footers + `/lr:doctor` holding 1 ailment) is the same shape — lots of machinery routing around a few real events. Question whether a much simpler migration model would do, and treat machinery-about-machinery as a cost center to minimize rather than polish. See `system-design-principles.md`, `versioning-release-types.md`.
194:- **`repos:` validators in `/lr:check`** — verify URL syntax (parseable, has scheme), reachability (probe with `git ls-remote`, run sparingly because network), dir-name collision (two declared URLs deriving the same dir name across descriptors). Not added in v11 by design; v25 added unsafe URL rejection in the script but not the full check suite. Trigger: user reports stale or broken URL lists; multi-domain workspaces accumulate friction. See `v25-workspace-pull-init-design.md`.
```
