242:- **Evaluate OKF compatibility/adoption for lore topics.** Google announced OKF v0.1 (2026-06-12, Apache 2.0): a vendor-neutral spec for knowledge as markdown + YAML frontmatter in git-hostable directories, linked into a traversable graph — a standards-level move in exactly our substrate space. If it gains traction, "OKF-compatible lore" could be a cheap interop/adoption win (any OKF-consuming agent could read a lore repo). **Known conflict:** OKF requires frontmatter (`type` field) on every knowledge file; our lore topics are deliberately frontmatter-free (frontmatter only on descriptor files, per `conventions.md`). Options to weigh: (a) adopt minimal frontmatter on topics — a real convention change; (b) an export/bridge script (`lore → OKF bundle`) keeping our format canonical; (c) watch and do nothing until OKF shows real consumer traction. Read the actual spec first (single page, per announcement) — the survey worked from secondary coverage. No action until evaluated; don't let a v0.1 spec drive a convention change. See `similar-projects-landscape.md` § Standards-level developments.

It's co-located for this plugin (source `"./"`), but co-location isn't guaranteed for all install/cache layouts (a marketplace-install cache may carry only `plugin.json`). Make the `marketplace.json` half conditional on the file existing, rather than erroring. Low-severity `/code-review` finding, shipped as-is. Analysis done — I pulled the prior positions from my lore (the parked boot-context-cache item, the 2026-07-18 review verdict, the consciousness/substrate principle) and measured the actual boot path, including the one I just executed. Here's my assessment.

## Verdict

Yes — but overcomplicated in a *specific, diagnosable* way, not overall. The boot's judgment content is near zero; it's ~90% deterministic choreography executed as prose by the LLM. That's the expensive combination: slow (sequential tool round-trips), token-costly (procedure prose in context), and fidelity-fragile (haiku-tier models misexecute prose steps — the lifecycle harness exists largely to catch exactly this). My own lore already converged on the diagnosis from two directions:

- The 2026-07-18 architecture review verdict: **"mechanical procedures executed as LLM instructions don't hold at current scale"** (that's item A1 in `what-to-improve.md` — script-back `/lr:check`'s mechanical subset).
- The Lore Beings principle: **consciousness = LLM, substrate = deterministic code, never a model** (`agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`). Boot preflight is substrate.

The core design principle "agent-driven operations, no shell scripts" already carries the exception — *pure mechanical operations requiring no judgment use scripts delegated from skills* — and `scripts/` already has precedent (`workspace-pull`, `lrb.py`, `session-takeover`). So this is re-scoping within the existing principle, not violating it.

## Where the cost actually is

This session's boot: **9 tool calls across ~6 sequential rounds**, and ~450 lines of procedure prose loaded (`agent-boot.md` 103 + `auto-pull.md` 96 + engine profile 27–186 + `version-check.md` 162 when versions differ). Of that, only two reads were the *actual agent* — `role.md` + `lore-context.md`. Everything else (engine detection, discovery, git preflight, version compare, `ps` teammate detection) is deterministic and encodes traps we've repeatedly paid for in prose: the macOS `timeout` binary trap, the `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT`/`BatchMode` dance, the `ps -o args=` header trap, the multi-field single-line `ps` bug. A script kills that whole bug class.

The worse multiplier is **fan-out**: merge spawns one booted subagent per agent, consult/attach boot fresh — each re-executes this full prose choreography, including a redundant `git pull` of a repo pulled minutes earlier.

## What I'd do (ranked)

1. **`scripts/boot-preflight <agent-dir>`** — one script doing: engine detection, agent discovery, auto-pull (all safety gates baked in), version comparison, teammate detection → compact JSON report. `agent-boot.md` shrinks to roughly: *run preflight, read its report, read `role.md` + `lore-context.md`, apply engine profile, confirm*. Boot drops to ~3 tool calls / 2 rounds; `auto-pull.md` leaves the boot read-path entirely. Python3 stdlib per the `lrb.py` precedent (portable-shell rules out GNU-dependent bash), degraded-mode semantics preserved: script failure = warn and continue, exactly today's contract. Sandboxed engines (Codex no-network, Cursor no-`ps`) degrade gracefully — same as today, but reported honestly in the JSON.
2. **Pull-freshness TTL in that script** — stamp last-pull time (e.g. under `.git/` or a gitignored cache file), skip the pull if fresh within ~10 min. This is the *right* version of caching here: it accelerates exactly the painful path (merge/consult/attach/recall fan-outs re-pulling the same repo), respects the freshness contract at real session boundaries, and costs ~10 lines.
3. **`scripts/finalize-git`** for finalize phase 4 — the commit/push choreography (scoped adds per touched repo, commit message, `--ff-only` push, conflict *detection*) is fully mechanical. The LLM keeps the review gate and conflict *resolution* (that's judgment). This is the only finalize phase worth scripting aggressively.
4. **Summarize scaffolding** — `docs/summarize.md` is 413 lines, the fattest doc in the lifecycle. The UUID/path/frontmatter mechanics could be a tiny scaffold script; the narrative stays LLM. Lower priority — it's one call per session, not a fan-out.

## What NOT to move into scripts

- **Reflect and merge content** — deciding what's worth keeping and how it integrates into the lore graph *is* the product. Any scripting there is theater.
- **The parked boot-context cache** (backlog, 2026-06-05) — I'd leave it parked, and this analysis is the reason: the expensive part of boot is procedure execution, not the two file Reads. `role.md` + `lore-context.md` must enter context regardless; a prebuilt cache saves one Read and adds an invalidation mechanism to maintain. Preflight-script + TTL captures ~all the value at ~none of the machinery. (This also serves the backlog's own "machinery-about-machinery is a cost center" flag.)

## Gates before shipping

This touches the most-executed procedure doc in the framework, so both standing gates apply in full: `/lr:trilens-loop` to convergence on the doc changes, and the **complete lifecycle suite on all three engines at the cheap tiers** (boot scenarios are precisely what the harness covers). Notably, the script should *raise* the haiku-fidelity floor, not risk it — fewer prose steps to misexecute is the whole point.

My recommendation: ship #1+#2 as one release (they're one script), #3 as a second. This also composes with A1 (script-backed `/lr:check`) — same pattern, and plausibly the same release train. Want me to draft the preflight script + the slimmed `agent-boot.md` as a concrete design, or discuss any of the calls above first?

- model: `claude-fable-5`

You've talked yourself to the right answer, and it's worth stating precisely *why* it's right: what you're describing — "at finalize, prepare a precomputed context file so later agents boot boosted" — is not something the framework happens to approximate, it's the framework's core loop by design. `lore-context.md` **is** that precached file. Reflect → merge exists precisely to transform raw session experience into the compacted artifact the next boot loads in one Read. Commit+push then distributes it to the whole team, and auto-pull at the next boot picks it up. So the "prepare data for future agents" step is already first-class, and the answer to "nothing to optimize here?" is: nothing *structural*, correct.

Two residual angles, briefly, since I checked them rather than assuming:

**Packaging optimization — not worth it.** The only mechanical gain left would be merging `role.md` + `lore-context.md` into a single generated `boot-bundle.md` at finalize so boot does one Read instead of two. I'd reject that: it saves one tool call but creates a second copy of the same content, which violates single-canonical-source and introduces a drift/invalidation problem (hand-edits to `role.md` between finalizes would need bundle regeneration). Same reasoning that keeps the parked boot-context-cache item parked — the two Reads aren't the cost; the prose choreography around them is, and the preflight script from our previous discussion already takes that.

**Quality optimization — this is the real lever, and it's ongoing.** The value of the "precached file" is entirely in its curation, and that's governed by the shape discipline (`lore-context-shape-discipline.md`: working-knowledge + summary-topic references, ≤50K tokens, not an index). A bloated or index-shaped lore-context is the one way boot actually gets slow and expensive in a way no script can fix — you'd be paying input tokens for a bad cache every session. So the finalize-time investment that "boosts later agents" is merge quality, which is also why post-merge diff verification (B1 on the standing list) is ranked where it is.

One small conceptual link worth keeping: your framing makes explicit that **finalize and boot are two halves of one cache protocol** — merge is the cache *write*, boot is the cache *read*, git is the transport, and auto-pull is the coherence mechanism. That's a nice named framing for the docs someday, but operationally: nothing to build here.

```text
1	# Searching Your Lore
2	
3	Read this when you need to find or recall topics from your lore. Covers both the agent-initiated case (you decide you need lore) and the user-triggered case (`/lr:recall`). Also covers the multi-agent case when guests are attached to the host session.
4	
5	Default scope is `agents/<agent>/lore/` only. Do not include `sessions/`, `archive/`, or other agent subdirectories in ordinary lore recall. Search/read `archive/` only when the user explicitly asks for full session logs or a particular session, or when the task specifically requires session-log evidence.
6	
7	## Preferred Mechanism: Subagent Scan
8	
9	For any non-trivial search, **dispatch an `Explore` subagent** with a structured search brief. The subagent reads relevant lore topics directly, synthesizes findings, and returns a compact result. This keeps your main context clean and uses the LLM's own semantic understanding — stronger than keyword matching for finding topics by *meaning* rather than by *exact term*.
10	
11	> **Engine note.** "`Explore` subagent" and "single-message parallel dispatch" describe Claude Code. If your engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`) defines a subagent-spawn override, use its read-only spawn instead — e.g. on Codex, `spawn_agent` with role `explorer`, gathered via `wait_agent`.
12	
13	Why a subagent:
14	- Reading 10–100 topic files in your main context wastes tokens you need for the user's task
15	- The synthesis can be inlined cheaply (a few hundred words)
16	- `Explore` is fast and tool-restricted (Read/Grep/Glob, no edit/agent tools) — exactly what's needed for a search
17	
18	## Active Agents: Fan Out When Guests Are Attached
19	
20	If guests are attached to the host session (via `/lr:attach`), the search runs across **all active agents' lore directories** — host + every attached guest. Execute this by dispatching **one `Explore` subagent per active agent in parallel** (all subagent calls in a single message), each scoped to one agent's `lore/` directory.
21	
22	Each subagent receives the same brief but is told which agent's lore to scan. Each returns its own compact synthesis. The host merges the results for the user, grouped by agent so it's clear whose lore each finding came from.
23	
24	When there are no guests, the fan-out reduces to a single subagent — behavior is identical to the single-agent case. You don't need to branch your procedure; just enumerate the active agents (host plus any attached guests) and dispatch N subagents.
25	
26	**Why parallel, not serial:** each subagent runs independently and there are no dependencies between them. Single-message parallel dispatch is both faster and the standard pattern for independent subagent work.
27	
28	## How to Prepare a Search Brief
29	
30	The quality of what comes back depends entirely on the brief. A good brief has four parts:
31	
32	### 1. The Task — One or Two Sentences
33	
34	What you're actively working on. Be concrete:
35	
36	- Good: *"Refactoring the framework update process to support a release-notes track alongside migrations."*
37	- Bad: *"Working on framework stuff."*
38	
39	### 2. Session Context That Shapes Relevance
40	
41	Two to five bullets covering decisions made, constraints surfaced, paths or filenames being touched, open questions. This is what lets the subagent distinguish *relevant* from *generally related*.
42	
43	### 3. The Angle — If You Have One
44	
45	If the user provided a hint (`/lr:recall anything about versioning`), center the brief on it.
46	
47	If not, describe the *kind* of lore that would help — prior decisions, operational lessons, domain facts, known pitfalls, past trade-offs. This anchors the subagent's reading.
48	
49	### 4. The Output Shape You Want Back
50	
51	Ask explicitly for:
52	- A compact synthesis (under ~400 words) grouped by theme
53	- Each finding tagged with its source topic filename
54	- Explicit call-outs for topics worth reading in full yourself
55	- *"Nothing relevant found"* if that's the honest answer — don't invent relevance
56	
57	Keep the whole brief under ~300 words. The subagent has access to your full `lore/` directory; you don't need to summarize what's *in* lore, only what's in *this session*.
58	
59	## Worked Example
60	
61	**Brief sent to the subagent:**
62	
63	> Search the lore at `agents/lore-architect/lore/` for topics relevant to my current task.
64	>
65	> **Task:** Designing a `/lr:recall` skill that lets the user explicitly trigger a lore search mid-session. The synthesis is inlined into the main agent's working context.
66	>

88	
89	1. **Show the synthesis to the user.** Transparency — they should see what got pulled in and judge whether the recall was useful. If multiple active agents were scanned (fan-out), group results by agent so it's clear whose lore each finding came from.
90	2. **Carry it as working context** for the rest of the session.

94

96	
97	- **Glob / list** — see what topics exist
98	- **Grep** — search for a specific keyword across topics
99	- **Read** — read a specific topic in full
100	- **`git log`** — when a topic was created (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log --diff-filter=A -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`), last updated (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log -1 -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`), or full history (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log --follow -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`). Use `git -C` rather than `cd`ing into the repo — the shell CWD is shared with Glob and Grep.
101	- **Deleted topics** remain in git history — searchable if you need past knowledge that was later removed
102	
103	Rule of thumb: use the subagent when you're searching by **meaning**; use direct tools when you're searching by **name** or **exact term**.

Yes — a few more, but the sweep also shows most of the remaining surface is either already scripted or genuinely judgment-work. Here's the full map, ranked by value.

## Worth scripting

**1. Attach and consult — free riders on boot-preflight, if we design it right.** Attach's mechanical half is exactly boot's (auto-pull the guest repo, version-check it, read two files), and consult's subagent boots the consultant the same way. So the preflight script from our earlier discussion should take a repo/agent argument and make no host-vs-guest assumptions — then `attach.md` and `consult.md` shrink for free, and the pull-TTL cache pays off most *here* (consult/attach/merge fan-outs re-pulling repos that were pulled minutes ago). This is a design constraint on item #1, not a new build.

**2. A `lore-scan` manifest script for recall/search.** The semantic half of `lore-search.md` is correctly LLM (search by meaning; the Explore subagent stays). But there's a mechanical layer under it: enumerate topics, extract first lines, batch `git log -1` dates per topic. Today every recall subagent re-derives that map by listing and sampling files, and staleness is invisible. A script emitting a topic manifest — filename, first line, last-modified date, age flag — handed to the subagent as its reading map would cut recall latency, ship **B2 (staleness surfacing)** essentially for free, and produce the measurement data **B4 (semantic search trigger)** wants before we consider vector DBs. This is the highest *new* value on the list because recall is high-frequency.

**3. Discovery as a shared module: `list-agents`, `list-repos`, and boot Step 1.** All three do the same purely mechanical thing — scan workspace roots for `lore-repo.md`, read frontmatter, check shortcuts. A2 on the standing list already flags list-agents/list-repos for restructuring (thin-pointer violation); the fix should be script-backed rather than just moving prose to `docs/`. Same discovery code serves boot-preflight.

**4. Scaffolding/templating: `register-agent`/`register-repo` (161 lines) and the skeleton half of `create-agent`/`create-repo`.** Pure template generation, and `sync-cursor-skills` is the precedent. Role *content* drafting stays LLM. Lower priority only because these run rarely — the token/latency cost isn't felt.

Plus the already-agreed anchors: `/lr:check`'s mechanical subset (A1) and finalize phase 4. One structural suggestion: rather than five scattered scripts, ship **one python3-stdlib CLI with subcommands** (`preflight`, `scan`, `discover`, `check`, `finalize-git`) — the `lrb.py` model. One file to port-test per engine, one place for the shared discovery/git code, and sandbox degradation handled uniformly.

## Deliberately not scripting

- **`trilens-loop` (325 lines)** — the orchestration *is* semantics (reviewer independence, the ledger, convergence judgment); scripting spawns would fight the per-engine subagent bindings.
- **`update`/migrations** — migrations are prose executed with judgment; only version *detection* is mechanical, and preflight already covers that.
- **`doctor`** — ailment matching is fuzzy by design.
- **reflect/merge content, summarize narrative, workspace-init dialogue** — the product.
- **Already deterministic:** `workspace-pull`, `session-takeover`, `sync-cursor-skills`, `lrb` (Beings), `lr-wait`. The Beings Keeper is in fact the pattern we're extending backward into the older skills.

So the consolidated build order across all three conversations: **(1)** boot-preflight + pull-TTL, designed guest-capable so attach/consult inherit it; **(2)** lore-scan manifest (recall + B2 + B4 data); **(3)** finalize-git; **(4)** discovery-backed list skills (A2) and check (A1) on the same core; **(5)** scaffolding scripts opportunistically. Each ship gates on trilens convergence + the full lifecycle suite, since these are the most-executed procedure docs.

Want me to draft the CLI design (subcommand surface, JSON contracts, degraded-mode semantics) as a workdir design doc?

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""lrb — the Being Keeper: CLI + deterministic daemon for Lore Beings.

Part of the lore-framework plugin (`lr`). Pure Python 3 standard library, no
pip install, one file — same sanctioned-exception class as
`scripts/wait-server.py` (see docs/beings.md and the design draft this
implements, `lore-framework-dev/agents/lore-architect/workdir/draft-lore-beings.md`).

A "being" is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor
(`agents/<name>/being.md`) declaring existential tasks on a cron schedule.
The Keeper is the deterministic supervisor that spawns headless engine
sessions on schedule, enforces budget (daily-USD spawn gate + per-task
wall-clock kill), and tracks state — it never reasons, never judges; that's
the being's job once spawned. See `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`.

Machine home: $LRB_HOME, default ~/.lore-beings/ (config.json + this script's
installed copy). Workspace state: <workspace>/.lr-beings/ (state.json, outbox/,
logs/) — gitignored, per-workspace.

CLI:
  lrb install                          copy self to $LRB_HOME, write launchd plist
                                        (--launchd to load it)
  lrb status [--json]                  beings, last runs, spend, failures
  lrb validate                         static config/descriptor checks
  lrb logs BEING                       ledger/log pointers for one being
  lrb pause / lrb resume                all-beings scheduling switch (dead-man file)
  lrb stop                             SIGTERM running sessions + pause
  lrb engines add|remove|list          explicit engine configuration (§7 of draft)
  lrb workspaces add|remove|list       workspace registry
  lrb schedule --agent A --at ISO [--timeout-minutes N] "prompt"   outbox one-shot
  lrb daemon [--once]                  run the tick loop (what launchd/testing invoke)

Floor: Python 3.9. No `match`, no `X | Y` annotations.
"""

import argparse
import json
import math
import os
import re
=== workspace-pull head ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# workspace-pull — pull fresh workspace state across two levels of repo declarations.
#
# Phases:
#   0  Pull the workspace root itself, if it is a git repo with an origin remote
#      (dirty-tree guarded, never fatal).
#   1  Read <workspace>/lore-workspace.md `repos:` (workspace-level layout) and clone
#      any declared repos not yet present.
#   2  Discover every <subdir>/lore-repo.md, merge each `repos:` (domain-level
#      dependencies), and clone any not already satisfied by phase 1.
#   3  When the workspace root is git-tracked, append a `/child/` line to .gitignore
#      for every declared repo present on disk (idempotent; never auto-commits).
#   4  Pull every top-level git repo (existing + freshly cloned) in parallel.
#
# Phase 1 must complete before phase 2: domain descriptors live inside the
# workspace-level agent repos, so those repos must be on disk before discovery.
#
# Usage: workspace-pull [WORKSPACE_DIR]
#
# Exit codes:
#   0  success — nothing to do, or every clone/pull succeeded with no conflicts
#   1  one or more clone/pull failures or conflicts requiring manual resolution
#   2  invalid invocation (workspace dir missing or not a directory)
#
# Phase 0 warnings (dirty tree, network) never set exit 1 on their own.
#
# Compatibility: written for Bash 3.2+ (default on macOS) — no associative arrays.

set -uo pipefail

```text
1	# Attach
2	
3	`/lr:attach` loads another lore agent into the currently booted host session so the host can work with the union of both agents' knowledge over many turns. This is the heavyweight option in the cross-agent-collaboration trio (recall / consult / attach); use it when the task genuinely spans two (or more) domains (different agent repos) and you'll be thinking in both across many turns.
4	
5	## Usage
6	
7	- `/lr:attach <agent-name>` — attach a guest agent
8	- `/lr:attach` (no arguments) — list currently attached guests
9	
10	## Concepts
11	
12	- **Host** — the agent originally booted via `/lr:boot` or a registered per-agent shortcut (`/lr-<name>-agent` on Claude Code, `$lr-<name>-agent` on Codex). Exactly one per session. The host is the sole executor.
13	- **Guest** — an agent attached into the host session. Zero or more per session. Guests are knowledge loads — they extend what the host knows and can do, without becoming separate executors.
14	- **Active agents** — host + all currently attached guests. This is the set that `/lr:recall` fans out over and that `/lr:reflect` / `/lr:merge` / `/lr:finalize` iterate over.
15	
16	The session is single-executor, multi-personality: the host stays in charge, the guests contribute knowledge.
17	
18	## With no arguments — list attached guests
19	
20	If `$ARGUMENTS` is empty:
21	
22	1. Identify active agents from the conversation. The host is whichever agent was booted via `/lr:boot` or a registered per-agent shortcut. Guests are any agents that were confirmed as attached by prior `/lr:attach` commands in this session and have not been removed (detach is not supported in v1, so they all stay).
23	2. Print the active-agents state:
24	   - `Host: <host-name> — <one-line role.md description>`
25	   - For each guest: `Guest: <guest-name> — <one-line role.md description>`
26	   - If no guests: `No guests attached.`
27	3. Do not touch disk. Stop.
28	
29	## With an agent name — attach a guest
30	
31	### Step 1: Preconditions
32	
33	1. **Host must be loaded.** If no agent was booted in this session, respond: `No agent loaded. Run /lr:boot <agent-name> first, then /lr:attach.` and stop.
34	2. **Target must exist.** Run the standard agent discovery: scan all directories in the working directory for `lore-repo.md` files; within each, look for `agents/<target>/role.md`. If not found, list all available agents across all lore agent repos and stop with an error.
35	3. **Target must not be the host.** If the requested name equals the host, respond: `<name> is already the host — use /lr:recall to search its lore.` and stop.
36	4. **Target must not already be a guest.** If the requested name is already attached, respond: `<name> is already attached.` and stop (idempotent).
37	
38	### Step 2: Auto-pull the guest repo
39	
40	Run the procedure in `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md` scoped to `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`. Best-effort — a pull failure never blocks the attach. Pull before version-reconcile so the reconcile sees the freshest `lore-repo.md`.
41	
42	### Step 3: Version reconcile in a subagent
43	
44	> **Engine note.** The subagent path below describes Claude Code. If your engine profile
45	> (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`) defines a subagent-spawn override, use it here
46	> too — e.g. on Cursor, run the version reconcile **inline in the host context**, scoped to the
47	> guest repo, rather than dispatching a general-purpose subagent.
48	
49	Read the target repo's `lore-repo.md` and extract its `version` field. Compare with the contents of `<framework-root>/VERSION` (trimmed).
50	
51	- If they match, skip to Step 4.
52	- If they differ, dispatch a general-purpose subagent to reconcile. The subagent works in the filesystem — its output stays in its own context; the host only sees the subagent's summary return.
53	
54	Subagent prompt shape:
55	
56	> Reconcile the lore agent repo at `<guest-lore-agent-repo>` to the installed framework version.
57	>
58	> Read `<framework-root>/docs/version-check.md` and execute its procedure, scoped to this repo. The repo's current version is `R=<R>` and the framework version is `F=<F>`.
59	>
60	> **Deviation from version-check.md:** do not print release notes to the user directly — instead, collect the full text of each release notes file you would have displayed, and return it in your response so the host can surface it to the user.
61	>
62	> Return a compact report containing:
63	> - Start version, end version, final stamped version (or "not stamped" if the upgrade deferred or failed)
64	> - List of migrations applied (filenames only)
65	> - Full contents of any release notes files that should be shown to the user
66	> - Any warnings (uncommitted changes, version > framework, migration errors)
67	> - Degraded-mode status (did the upgrade succeed cleanly, defer, or fail)
68	
69	After the subagent returns:
70	
71	- **Relay release notes to the user** verbatim. They are meant for user eyes.
72	- **Surface any warnings** — uncommitted-changes defer, version-above-framework warning, or migration failure.
73	- **If the upgrade failed or deferred**, continue the attach in degraded mode (version-skew warning visible) — matches boot-time behavior. Do not block the attach on a failed upgrade.
74	
75	### Step 4: Load guest context
76	
77	In the host's main context, read:
78	
79	1. `agents/<guest>/role.md` — frontmatter has `description`; the body defines the guest's identity and responsibilities.
80	2. `agents/<guest>/lore-context.md` — the guest's compacted working knowledge (≤50K tokens).
81	
82	These files now live in the host's working context alongside the host's own role and lore-context.
83	
84	### Step 5: Confirm
85	
86	Print a confirmation message. Include:
87	
88	- `Attached <guest-name>.`
89	- Active agents line: `Active agents: host=<host-name>, guests=[<g1>, <g2>, ...]`.
90	- One-line summary of the guest's role (taken from the guest's `role.md` frontmatter description).
91	- If a version reconcile ran, note it: `Guest repo was upgraded from <R> to <F>.`
92	
93	This confirmation message is the host's record of the attachment — the session conversation itself tracks active agents. No disk state is written for the attach.
94	
95	## Conflict resolution
96	
97	When the guest's lore disagrees with the host's (different conventions, different recommendations, different "always do X" rules), the **host's knowledge takes precedence**. The guest's perspective is visible and can inform judgment, but the host's own lore governs action. This keeps host identity stable and avoids silent harmonization.
98	
99	In practice: note the disagreement to the user if it's material, then follow the host's rule unless the user overrides.
100	
101	## What changes after attach
102	
103	Subsequent operations become multi-agent-aware automatically:
104	
105	- **`/lr:recall` fans out** — one parallel subagent per active agent, results synthesized together. See `docs/recall.md` and `docs/lore-search.md`.
106	- **`/lr:reflect` / `/lr:merge` / `/lr:finalize` iterate** — sequentially, per active agent, in host-first order. See `docs/process-reflection.md` and `docs/process-merge.md`.
107	- **Workdir writes** default to the host's workdir. Guests' workdirs are readable (workspace visibility already gives the host this).
108	
109	No detach in v1. Guests stay attached for the rest of the session and participate in finalization.
110	
111	## Attaching multiple guests
112	
113	Call `/lr:attach` multiple times. Order matters only for finalization iteration (host first, then guests in attach order). There is no hard cap on the number of guests — token cost is the user's responsibility. Each guest's `lore-context.md` can be up to 50K tokens; attaching several visibly shrinks the host's working budget.
114	
115	## Escalation from consult
116	
117	If a `/lr:consult <agent>` surfaces that you actually need sustained engagement with that agent's knowledge, call `/lr:attach <same-agent>` — this is the clean transition from one-shot to loaded.
118	
119	## See also
120	
121	- `<framework-root>/docs/consult.md` — the one-shot sibling (subagent boots, answers, exits; no host-side loading)
122	- `<framework-root>/docs/recall.md` — lore search across active agents
123	- `<framework-root>/docs/lore-search.md` — search brief structure, fan-out mechanics
124	- `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md` — the per-repo refresh procedure invoked at Step 2
125	- `<framework-root>/docs/version-check.md` — migration procedure used by the Step 3 subagent
126	- `<framework-root>/docs/pull-lore.md` — `/lr:pull-lore` does the same auto-pull mid-session for already-attached agents
127	- `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and `process-merge.md` — per-agent iteration when guests are attached
128	
```

#### Result

````text
1	# Version Check
2	
3	Invoked from `agent-boot.md` when the agent's repo `version` differs from the framework `VERSION`. Reconciles the booting agent's repo to the current framework version automatically.
4	
5	## Inputs
6	
7	- `R` — `version` field from the booting agent's repo `lore-repo.md`
8	- `F` — contents of `<framework-root>/VERSION` (trimmed)
9	
10	## Cases
11	
12	### `R == F`
13	
14	You should not be reading this doc — `agent-boot.md`'s version check skipped this file. Treat as a no-op and continue boot.
15	
16	### `R > F`
17	
18	The repo is stamped at a version newer than the installed framework. The plugin may be out of date.
19	
20	- Print a warning to the user. Use the active engine profile to give the engine-specific next step:
21	  - **Codex:** `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — your Codex plugin is older than the repo. Refresh the plugin (`codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`; if the marketplace is Git-backed, run `codex plugin marketplace upgrade lore-framework` first), restart Codex, then boot again. See <framework-root>/INSTALL-CODEX.md.`
22	  - **Cursor:** `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — your Cursor plugin/session is older than the repo. Refresh the plugin source per your install method, start a fresh Cursor session, then boot again. See <framework-root>/INSTALL-CURSOR.md.`
23	  - **Other engines:** `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — your plugin may be out of date. Refresh or reinstall the plugin using your engine's normal install flow, then boot again.`
24	- Do NOT modify any files.
25	- Continue boot in degraded mode.
26	
27	### `R < F`
28	
29	The repo is behind. Run the upgrade procedure below.
30	
31	## Upgrade Procedure
32	
33	### Step 1: Pre-flight collision check
34	
35	A blanket "any uncommitted change blocks the upgrade" rule was the historical gate, but it overfired in normal use: lore agent repos routinely carry uncommitted runtime state (other agents' `workdir/*` files — pulse logs, watch markers, scratch artifacts) that cannot collide with what the upgrade writes. The gate is now scoped to **actual collisions** — files git would refuse to overwrite cleanly when the upgrade applies its writes — and to **structural inconsistencies** (conflict markers).
36	
37	Run `git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" status --porcelain` (quote the substituted path so it survives spaces; use `git -C` rather than `cd`ing — the shell CWD is shared with Glob, Grep, and subsequent git calls, so a stray `cd` silently shifts their root for the rest of the session). Untracked files (`??`) are ignored throughout — the upgrade never overwrites a file git doesn't already track.
38	
39	#### 1a. Conflict markers — always defer
40	
41	`git status --porcelain` exposes two distinct signals of an in-progress merge:
42	
43	- **Unmerged status codes:** `UU`, `AA`, `DD`, `AU`, `UA`, `UD`, `DU` — git itself is reporting the file as actively conflicted. Treat any such code as a hard-defer condition without further inspection.
44	- **Modified files containing conflict markers:** for files marked with the modified codes (`M `, ` M`, `MM`, `AM`, `MD`, `RM`, `CM`, etc. — anything where the working-tree side is `M`), scan for unresolved markers.
45	
46	The marker scan must use **precise anchors** to avoid false-positives on legitimate Markdown:
47	
48	- The string `=======` at the start of a line matches setext H1 underlines (`Title\n=======`) — extremely common in lore docs. **Do not** flag a file on `=======` alone.
49	- Require **all three markers** (`<<<<<<<`, `=======`, `>>>>>>>`) to appear in the same file, each anchored to the start of a line and followed by a space or end-of-line. The git-emitted form is `<<<<<<< <ref>`, `=======` (alone), `>>>>>>> <ref>`. Concretely:
50	
51	  ```
52	  grep -lE '^<<<<<<< ' <file> >/dev/null \
53	    && grep -lE '^=======($| )' <file> >/dev/null \
54	    && grep -lE '^>>>>>>> ' <file> >/dev/null
55	  ```
56	
57	  All three must match the same file. If any one of the three is absent, the file is not in a real conflict state — likely it's a doc that quotes one or two markers as documentation (this very release's `release-notes/15.md` quotes all three; the framework's `docs/resolve-conflicts.md` and lore topics about merge handling do too — these are not conflicts).
58	
59	If any unmerged-state file (any of `UU`/`AA`/`DD`/`AU`/`UA`/`UD`/`DU`) **or** any modified file matching the all-three-markers test is found:
60	
61	- Print: `<lore-agent-repo>: cannot auto-upgrade from R to F — unresolved merge-conflict markers in <file(s)>. Resolve the conflict and commit before booting again.`
62	- Do NOT modify any files. Continue boot in degraded mode. **This deferral is NOT a boot failure** — return to `agent-boot.md` and finish loading the agent; do not emit any boot-failed signal or stop the boot.
63	
64	This is the only **always-defer** condition. A conflict-marker file is structurally broken regardless of upgrade scope.
65	
66	#### 1b. Compute the upgrade write-set
67	
68	Build the set of repo-relative paths the upgrade is about to write:
69	
70	1. Always include `lore-repo.md` (the final version stamp in Step 3).
71	2. For each `v` in `R+1 .. F`:
72	   - If `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists, look for a `## Write Paths` section (see `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths). The section content is **only** what lies inside the first fenced code block (` ``` … ``` `) immediately following the `## Write Paths` heading. Any prose paragraphs after the closing fence belong to the next subsection, not to the write-set — they are commentary for human readers and the parser MUST NOT consume them. If no fenced block is present between the `## Write Paths` heading and the next `## ` (or `### `) heading, the section is malformed — treat as missing (fall back to blanket-dirty, see below) and warn the user.
73	
74	     Inside the fenced block, parse the lines using these rules **in order**:
75	     1. **Strip blank lines and comment lines** (lines starting with `#`, or having `# comment` after a path — strip the trailing comment).
76	     2. **Sentinel detection**: if any remaining line matches the `(none)` sentinel as defined in `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths § *Empty write-sets — sentinel forms* (the canonical grammar — do not restate the accepted forms here), this is the **explicit empty write-set sentinel**. Contribute nothing for this version. Do **not** treat `(none)` as a glob, and do **not** fall back to blanket-dirty.
77	     3. **Glob lines**: every other remaining line is a repo-relative glob — add to the write-set.
78	     4. **Zero remaining lines** (after blanks/comments stripped, and no `(none)` sentinel) is also an explicit empty write-set — same treatment as the sentinel.
79	   - If a migration exists but the `## Write Paths` **section is absent entirely** (the heading itself is missing), treat its write-set as **unknown** — fall back to the conservative gate: defer on **any** dirty tracked file (the historical behavior, but only for this version range, not as a global default). Note: an empty fenced block is *not* the same as a missing section — the section's presence with empty content is an explicit declaration of "writes nothing" and proceeds without deferral.
80	   - If only `release-notes/<v>.md` exists for that version (no migration), it contributes nothing to the write-set — release-notes-only versions don't write to user repos.
81	
82	#### 1c. Intersect dirty tracked files with the write-set
83	
84	Match each dirty tracked file's repo-relative path against the write-set globs.
85	
86	- **Empty intersection** (no dirty file collides) → proceed to Step 2.
87	
88	- **Exact `lore-repo.md`-only collision on a stamp-only upgrade** → proceed to Step 2. If the
89	  colliding set is exactly `lore-repo.md`, and the computed write-set is also exactly
90	  `lore-repo.md` (that is: no migration in `R+1 .. F` writes any repo file beyond the final
91	  version stamp), do **not** defer. This is the common "release-notes-only versions plus a dirty
92	  stamp file" case. It is safe to continue because Step 3 reads the current working-tree
93	  `lore-repo.md` and updates only its `version` field while preserving the rest of the file.
94	  This also covers a previously-deferred local stamp such as committed `17`, working tree `18`,
95	  now advancing to `23`: the earlier dirty stamp is not a reason to block the later stamp-only
96	  upgrade.
97	
98	- **Non-empty intersection** → defer. Print a precise message naming the colliding files and the right command. Substitute `<lore-agent-repo>` with the actual repo path and quote it if it contains spaces — emit literal commands, not template placeholders:
99	
100	  ```
101	  <lore-agent-repo>: cannot auto-upgrade from R to F — uncommitted changes in files the upgrade would write:
102	    <file-1>
103	    <file-2>
104	    ...
105	  Resolve by either committing the changes:
106	    git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" commit <files>
107	  or reverting them:
108	    git -C "<lore-agent-repo>" checkout -- <files>
109	  Then boot again or run /lr:update.
110	  ```
111	
112	  **This deferral is NOT a boot failure.** Print the message above, then return to `agent-boot.md` and finish loading the agent in degraded mode — do not emit any boot-failed signal or stop the boot.
113	
114	In deferred cases: do NOT modify any files; continue boot in degraded mode. **A deferred upgrade is not a boot failure** — return to `agent-boot.md` step 3 and proceed to step 4; the agent still loads.
115	
116	### Step 2: Walk versions from R+1 to F
117	
118	For each version `v` in `R+1` through `F` inclusive, in order:
119	
120	1. **Apply migration** if `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists. Read the doc and follow its instructions, scoped to the booting agent's repo. Migrations are idempotent and may modify files.
121	
122	2. **Display release notes** if `<framework-root>/release-notes/<v>.md` exists. Print the contents to the user so they see what's new in this version.
123	
124	3. **At least one** must exist for version `v`. If neither `migrations/<v>.md` nor `release-notes/<v>.md` is present, this is a framework packaging bug — print an error, stop the upgrade for this repo, do NOT stamp the new version, continue boot in degraded mode.
125	
126	4. If a migration step fails, stop the upgrade for this repo. Do NOT apply any further versions. Do NOT stamp the new version. Print the failure with enough context for the user to investigate. Continue boot in degraded mode.
127	
128	### Step 3: Stamp the new version
129	
130	Only after all versions in step 2 succeed:
131	
132	1. Read `lore-repo.md` frontmatter from the booting agent's repo.
133	2. Update the `version` field to `F` (quoted string).
134	3. Write the file back, preserving the rest of the frontmatter and the body.
135	
136	### Step 4: Inform the user
137	
138	Print a brief summary:
139	- `<lore-agent-repo>: upgraded from R to F`
140	- The changes are uncommitted and ready for `git diff` review
141	
142	Then return to `agent-boot.md` and continue with reading `role.md` and `lore-context.md`.
143	
144	## Invariants
145	
146	- **Boot never fails on version errors.** Any failure here results in degraded-mode boot with a visible warning. The agent still loads.
147	- **No file the upgrade would write is overwritten while dirty.** The collision gate guarantees this — git's own write-time refusal is the second line of defense. Files the upgrade does *not* touch (other agents' `workdir/*`, unrelated scratch state) never block.
148	- **Conflict markers always defer.** A repo with unresolved conflict markers is structurally broken regardless of upgrade scope; the gate refuses uniformly.
149	- **Version stamps are atomic.** Either all migrations for the range succeed and the version is stamped, or no version is stamped — the next boot or `/lr:update` run can retry.
150	- **No commits.** The user reviews and commits the upgraded files themselves.
151	
152	## Relationship to `/lr:update`
153	
154	`/lr:update` is the user-triggered, manual entry point — it processes all repos in the workspace and supports `--dry-run`. The boot-time check described here is a per-repo automatic reconciliation that runs only for the booting agent's repo, with no dry-run and no user prompt. Both share the same migration/release-notes data and the same version-stamping logic.
155	
156	**Asymmetry note:** the **write-aware collision gate (Step 1) lives only on the boot-time path.** `/lr:update` is user-triggered — the user has direct visibility of the workspace state, can `git diff` before running, and accepts that running `/lr:update` against dirty files may overwrite them. The boot-time path is automatic and unattended, so it carries the gate. `/lr:update` writes through dirty files unconditionally; the user is expected to commit/stash first. (Bringing the gate to `/lr:update` is a possible future enhancement.)
157	
158	## For Framework Authors
159	
160	When writing a new `migrations/<N>.md`, **declare its write-set** under a `## Write Paths` section (see `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths). Without it, the boot-time gate falls back to the conservative blanket-dirty rule for the version range that includes your migration — meaning users with any unrelated dirty file will be blocked from auto-upgrading through your version. The declaration is mechanical (it's the same paths your migration's "Steps" section already touches) and the cost of omitting it is real friction.
161	
162	When changes touch plugin-cached state (skills, slash commands, scripts, SKILL.md-referenced docs), include the **Clear Plugin Cache** footer per `docs/conventions.md` § Migration / Release-Note Authoring. The cache-stale failure mode is invisible to the user until they invoke a missing skill — the footer makes the fix discoverable from the doc the user is already reading. See also `docs/doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md` for the underlying ailment.
163	
````

lore_dir\n\n    try:\n        names = sorted(n for n in os.listdir(lore_dir)\n                       if n.endswith(\".md\") and not n.startswith(\".\"))\n    except (IOError, OSError) as exc:\n        return None, str(exc)\n\n    repo = _repo_root_for(agent_dir)\n    dates = {}\n    if repo:\n        rel_dir = os.path.relpath(lore_dir, repo)\n        dates = git_last_modified(repo, rel_dir)\n\n    topics, undated, stale_count = [], 0, 0\n    for name in names:\n        path = os.path.join(lore_dir, name)\n        rel = os.path.relpath(path, repo) if repo else name\n        iso = dates.get(rel)\n        age = _age_days(iso)\n        if iso is None:\n            undated += 1\n        is_stale = age is not None and age > stale_days\n        if is_stale:\n            stale_count += 1\n        try:\n            size = os.path.getsize(path)\n        except (IOError, OSError):\n            size = None\n        topics.append({\n            \"file\": name,\n            \"title\": topic_title(path),\n            \"last_modified\": iso,\n            \"age_days\": age,\n            \"stale\": is_stale,\n            \"bytes\": size,\n        })\n\n    return {\n        \"lore_dir\": lore_dir,\n        \"count\": len(topics),\n        \"stale_days_threshold\": stale_days,\n        \"stale_count\": stale_count,\n        \"undated_count\": undated,\n        \"topics\": topics,\n    }, None\n\n\ndef _repo_root_for(agent_dir):\n    \"\"\"agents/<name>/ -> repo root, verified by lore-repo.md.\"\"\"\n    candidate = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(agent_dir)))\n    if os.path.isfile(os.path.join(candidate, \"lore-repo.md\")):\n        return candidate\n    return None\n\n\n# res.fail(\"no lore agent repos found in %s \"\n                 \"(no top-level directory contains lore-repo.md)\" % workspace)\n    return res\n\n\ndef _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace):\n 119	- Verbatim conversation excerpts
120	
121	## Tooling: CWD Safety
122	
123	The agent's shell working directory is shared across Bash, Glob, Grep, and subsequent git calls. A `cd` in one command silently shifts the root for every tool invocation that follows — the agent will not notice until a path resolves wrong. Symptoms: Glob returning "No files found" for files that clearly exist, or a repo-scoped git command pointed at the wrong repo.
124	
125	- **Prefer `git -C <lore-agent-repo> <cmd>`** over `cd <lore-agent-repo> && <cmd>` whenever a command is scoped to a particular repo. All framework docs follow this convention.
126	- **Never `cd` away from the workspace root** during a flow that iterates over repos (update, check, version-check). If you must, `cd` back before the next tool call.
127	- **Pass absolute paths to Glob** via the `path` parameter when you're unsure of the current CWD, rather than relying on pattern-relative resolution.
128	
129	## Tooling: Portable Shell
130	
131	Framework-authored shell commands run in whatever environment the agent booted in — and the primary dev platform is macOS (BSD userland), not GNU/Linux. Commands that silently assume GNU coreutils break there, and the failure is often invisible: a `command not found` (exit 127) inside a best-effort flow reads as the underlying operation failing, not as a portability bug.
132	
133	- **No GNU-only binaries.** Most notably `timeout` / `gtimeout` — absent on a stock macOS. To bound a command's runtime, use your Bash tool's own timeout parameter, not a `timeout` wrapper. (This exact trap silently disabled v13 auto-pull on macOS; see `auto-pull.md` § Step 2.) Inside a **standalone script** there is no Bash-tool timeout and no portable one-liner for a hard wall-clock cap — prevent hangs at the source with fail-fast env vars (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` with `BatchMode`/`ConnectTimeout`), or implement a bash watchdog (background the job, then `sleep`+`kill`). `scripts/workspace-pull` takes the fail-fast-env-vars route.
134	- **Watch BSD-vs-GNU flag differences** on tools present in both userlands: `sed -i` (BSD needs `-i ''`), `date -d` (GNU-only), `readlink -f` / `realpath` (not always present), `grep -P`, `xargs -r`. Prefer a portable invocation or a short Bash/`git` equivalent.
135	- **Prefer git's own knobs over external wrappers** for git operations: `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` (suppress HTTP(S) auth prompts), `GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=<n>'` (fail-fast SSH), `git -C <repo>` (see § Tooling: CWD Safety above).
136	
137	Same shape as CWD Safety: a portability landmine hidden inside an emitted shell command. When authoring or reviewing any framework doc or script that emits shell, assume BSD/macOS and check every command against this list.
138	
139	## Tooling: Non-Shell Runtimes (python3)
140	
141	Directly-runnable shipped scripts default to Bash-on-BSD (§ Tooling: Portable Shell). The exception is a **server component that must speak a protocol** where bash is impractical — most notably an **MCP stdio server** (newline-delimited JSON-RPC). Those may be written in **`python3`, standard library only** (no `pip install`), so they need no dependency install and run anywhere `python3` is present — any Linux, and any macOS with the Xcode Command Line Tools (a bare macOS may prompt to install them). `scripts/wait-server.py` is the first instance; it parallels the Node ULA module (`df/aiqa/workflows/ula-file-pass.js`) as a language-specific module the framework ships and drives, not a portable one-liner. Keep companion tooling in Bash where practical (e.g. `scripts/lr-emit`). Don't reach for `python3` for work a portable shell script can do.
142	
143	## Migration / Release-Note Authoring
144	
145	Every `migrations/<N>.md` and `release-notes/<N>.md` whose changes touch **plugin-cached state** must include a **Clear Plugin Cache** section near the top — right after the Summary, before the detailed "What's New" / "What Changed" body. The reader has just learned *that* something changed; the cache-clear is the single mandatory action and must not be buried at the bottom of a long doc where it is easy to miss. (`release-notes/12.md` and `13.md` are worked examples — footer hoisted directly under the Summary.)
146	
147	Triggers — include the footer if the version added, removed, renamed, or modified any of:
148

Same rules — stdlib only, no `pip`, one file, Python 3.9 floor.\n\n## Script Fallback Contract\n\nFramework scripts are **accelerators over canonical prose procedures, never replacements**. Every procedure a script implements is also specified in a `docs/` doc, and that doc — not the script — is normative. A script exists to spend one tool call where prose would spend six, and to encode platform traps once.\n\nThis makes script failure a recoverable condition rather than a dead end. When a doc delegates a step to a script and the script **fails to complete** — a non-zero exit other than a documented result code, a missing interpreter, no output, or output you cannot parse:\n\n1. **Notify the user immediately**, in one line: which script failed and that you are proceeding manually. Never fail silently, and never let a script failure look like the underlying operation failing.\n2. **Take over and execute the canonical prose procedure** the doc points to, resolving what you reasonably can along the way (a missing directory, a repo that needs a different path). The flow must reach the same end state it would have reached had the script worked.\n3. **Diagnose briefly, don't stall.** If the cause is obvious (no `python3`, non-executable file, wrong path), say so in one line. Otherwise finish the flow and report the failure at the end for follow-up. **Never abort the surrounding flow because a script died.**\n\n**Distinguish failure from a reported degraded condition.** A script that exits 0 while reporting a problem in its output (`\"pull\": {\"status\": \"failed\"}`, `\"teammate\": {\"verdict\": \"unknown\"}`) has *succeeded* — it did its job and told you the truth about the world. That is data: handle it per the procedure doc's degraded-mode guidance. No takeover, no user-facing script-failure notice.\n\nScripts covered by this contract state their exit-code contract in their own header. `scripts/lr-core` is the reference implementation: exit 0 = ran to completion (`ok: false` still means \"ran fine, request could not be satisfied\" — act on `errors`); exit 2 = could not complete, fall back.\n\n**Exemption: `scripts/lrb.py` (the Being Keeper).** The Keeper is substrate that must never be impersonated by a model — it enforces budget caps and kill-switches, and an LLM \"taking over manually\" would be exactly the prompt-theater the design forbids. Its failure mode is \"Keeper down, beings do not run\", not \"engine takes over\". No other script is exempt.\n\nDocs that delegate to a script carry **one line** pointing here — not a restatement of this contract (§ single canonical source).\n\n## Migration / Release-Note Authoring"
}

Some topics are **summary topics** that provide an overview of an area and link to more specific topics.\n\n### Searching Your Lore\n\n`lore-context.md` is a compressed index, not the full picture — treat it as a starting point, not the answer. At the start of any non-trivial task, scan your `lore/` directory for related topics before proceeding. Do **not** search `sessions/` or `archive/` as part of ordinary recall: `sessions/` contains summaries, and `archive/` contains full session logs that can be overwhelmingly large. Pass the output to the subagent as part of its brief (write it to a temp file and give the path if it is large; a 150-topic manifest is sizeable, and it belongs in the *subagent's* context, not yours).\n\nTwo things this buys beyond speed:\n\n- **Search by title, not just by filename.** The manifest exposes what each topic actually claims, so the subagent picks what to read on meaning rather than guessing from the name.\n- **Age-awareness.** Lore accumulates for years; a topic last touched 14 months ago may describe a superseded design. The boot procedure already auto-pulls — this step is defense-in-depth, both because the freshness contract belongs at the merge site explicitly (the moment when stale lore is most damaging) and to cover any boot-pull skip (no remote, network blip) that may have left the repo behind.
39:`--ff-only` is safe even though the merge subagent's working tree is dirty (the `reflections/` from phase 1, or any merge-in-progress edits): git refuses to fast-forward if the operation would clobber uncommitted edits, and otherwise advances `HEAD` cleanly leaving the working tree untouched. See `docs/auto-pull.md` § Invariants.
41:If auto-pull surfaces a non-fast-forward failure, do **not** abort merge — proceed in degraded mode and surface the warning in your return summary so the host can flag it. Concurrent finalize collisions are handled by the push-conflict resolution path in `resolve-conflicts.md`, not here.
```

"""Lifecycle scenarios 1-7: boot (catalog: workdir/draft-testing-pipeline.md).

import os
import shutil
import sys

import unittest

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))

    AGENT_NAME, BOOT_PROMPT, BOOT_UNKNOWN_PROMPT, CTX_CANARY, FRESH_CANARY,
    ROLE_CANARY, SKIP_REASON, break_origin, build_fixture, dirty_role_md, head,
    is_clean, make_origin_ahead, read_repo_version, run_engine,
)


@unittest.skipIf(SKIP_REASON, SKIP_REASON)
class BootScenarios(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        tmp = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="lr-lifecycle-")
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, tmp, ignore_errors=True)
        self.fx = build_fixture(tmp)

    def _boot(self):
        r = run_engine(self.fx.workspace, BOOT_PROMPT)
        print(f"\n  [{self.id().split('.')[-1]}] {r.summary()}")
        return r

    def _assert_booted(self, r):
        self.assertEqual(r.exit_code, 0, f"engine run failed: {r.stderr[-500:]}")
        self.assertNotIn("BOOT-FAILED", r.text, f"boot reported failure:\n{r.text}")
        self.assertIn(ROLE_CANARY, r.text, f"role.md canary missing:\n{r.text}")
        self.assertIn(CTX_CANARY, r.text, f"lore-context.md canary missing:\n{r.text}")

    def test_01_boot_happy_path(self):
        """Boot confirms role; role.md and lore-context.md actually read."""
        before = head(self.fx.repo)
        r = self._boot()
        self._assert_booted(r)
        self.assertEqual(head(self.fx.repo), before, "boot must not create commits")

    def test_02_boot_pulls_fresh_commits(self):
        """Origin is one commit ahead; boot auto-pull fast-forwards to it."""
        make_origin_ahead(self.fx)
        r = self._boot()
        self._assert_booted(r)
        self.assertEqual(
            head(self.fx.repo), head(self.fx.origin, "main"),
            "local repo not fast-forwarded to origin after boot",
        )
        fresh = os.path.join(self.fx.agent_dir, "lore", "fresh-fact.md")
        self.assertTrue(os.path.exists(fresh), "pulled topic file missing")
        with open(fresh) as f:
            self.assertIn(FRESH_CANARY, f.read())

    def test_03_boot_degraded_remote_unreachable(self):
        """Origin unreachable; auto-pull fails but boot still completes."""
        break_origin(self.fx)
        r = self._boot()
        self._assert_booted(r)

    def test_04_boot_unknown_agent(self):
        """Booting a nonexistent agent fails cleanly and lists real agents."""

@unittest.skipIf(SKIP_REASON, SKIP_REASON)
class BootUpgradeScenarios(unittest.TestCase):
    """Scenarios 5-6: version-mismatch auto-upgrade at boot time."""

    def setUp(self):
        self.tmp = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="lr-lifecycle-")
        self.addCleanup(shutil.rmtree, self.tmp, ignore_errors=True)

    def test_05_boot_version_mismatch_release_notes_only(self):
        """Repo one version behind (release-notes-only bump); boot upgrades and stamps it."""
        fx = build_fixture(self.tmp, version="17")
        r = run_engine(fx.workspace, BOOT_PROMPT)
        print(f"\n  [{self.id().split('.')[-1]}] {r.summary()}")
        self.assertEqual(r.exit_code, 0, f"engine run failed: {r.stderr[-500:]}")
        self.assertNotIn("BOOT-FAILED", r.text, f"boot reported failure:\n{r.text}")
        self.assertIn(ROLE_CANARY, r.text)
        self.assertIn(CTX_CANARY, r.text)
        self.assertEqual(
            read_repo_version(fx), harness.framework_version(),
            "lore-repo.md was not stamped to the current framework version",
        )
        self.assertFalse(
            is_clean(fx.repo),
            "version stamp must be left uncommitted for the user to review",
        )

    def test_06_boot_upgrade_gate_on_dirty_tree(self):
        """Repo far behind with a dirty file in the upgrade's write-set; boot defers, doesn't overwrite."""
        fx = build_fixture(self.tmp, version="1")
        dirty_role_md(fx)  # collides with migration 2's write-set: agents/*/role.md
        with open(os.path.join(fx.agent_dir, "role.md")) as f:
            dirty_content = f.read()

        r = run_engine(fx.workspace, BOOT_PROMPT)
        print(f"\n  [{self.id().split('.')[-1]}] {r.summary()}")
        self.assertEqual(r.exit_code, 0, f"engine run failed: {r.stderr[-500:]}")
        self.assertNotIn("BOOT-FAILED", r.text, f"boot reported failure:\n{r.text}")
        # Boot must still load the agent in degraded mode despite the deferred upgrade.
        self.assertIn(ROLE_CANARY, r.text)

        self.assertEqual(
            read_repo_version(fx), "1",
            "upgrade should have deferred (dirty-tree collision) — version must stay at 1",
        )
        with open(os.path.join(fx.agent_dir, "role.md")) as f:
            self.assertEqual(
                f.read(), dirty_content,
                "the dirty role.md must be left untouched when the upgrade defers",
            )

    def test_07_boot_repo_newer_than_framework_codex_guidance(self):
        """Codex warns with the engine-specific plugin refresh remedy when R > F."""
        if harness.ENGINE != "codex":
            self.skipTest("Codex-specific mismatch guidance scenario")

        expected_repo_version = str(int(harness.framework_version()) + 1)
        fx = build_fixture(self.tmp, version=expected_repo_version)

    """Run the engine headless in `workspace` and return a RunResult."""

        cmd = [

            "--plugin-dir", FRAMEWORK_DIR,
            "--model", MODEL,
            "--output-format", "json",
            "--dangerously-skip-permissions",
        ]
        proc = subprocess.run(
            cmd, cwd=workspace, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=RUN_TIMEOUT,
            env=env,
        )
    elif ENGINE == "cursor":
        cmd = [
            "cursor-agent", "-p", prompt,
            "--plugin-dir", FRAMEWORK_DIR,
            "--model", MODEL,
            "--output-format", "json",
            "--force",
            "--trust",
            "--sandbox", "disabled",
            "--workspace", workspace,
        ]
        proc = subprocess.run(
            cmd, cwd=workspace, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=RUN_TIMEOUT,
            env=env, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,
        )
    elif ENGINE == "codex":
        prompt = codex_prompt(prompt)
        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode="w+", encoding="utf-8", delete=False) as f:
            output_last_message = f.name
        try:
            cmd = [
                "codex", "exec", prompt,
                "-C", workspace,
                "--model", MODEL,
                "--skip-git-repo-check",
                "--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox",
                "--json",
                "--output-last-message", output_last_message,
            ]
            proc = subprocess.run(
                cmd, cwd=workspace, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=RUN_TIMEOUT,
                env=env, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,
            )
            text = proc.stdout
            if os.path.exists(output_last_message):
                with open(output_last_message, encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore") as fh:
                    text = fh.read() or proc.stdout
        finally:
            try:
                os.unlink(output_last_message)
            except FileNotFoundError:
                pass
        rr = RunResult(proc.returncode, text or "", None, None, proc.stderr)
        _debug_dump(rr)
        return rr
    else:

# Release Notes -- Version 30

## Summary

Version 30 adds **`/lr:trilens-loop`** — review a set of changes from three independent
perspectives, triage the findings, fix what matters, and repeat until the changes come back clean.
The three reviewers are subagents, so they do not carry the authoring session's bias toward its own
work.

It also corrects two engine profiles: Cursor's `subagent-spawn` binding (stale since Cursor shipped
subagents in 2.4) and Claude Code's (which never documented `fork` or the named-teammate
non-return trap).

## Clear Plugin Cache

This release **adds** a bundled skill (`skills/trilens-loop/SKILL.md`) and its SKILL-referenced doc
(`docs/trilens-loop.md`), adds the generated Cursor wrapper
(`.cursor-skills/lr-trilens-loop/SKILL.md`), and **modifies** `docs/engines/cursor.md` and
`docs/engines/claude.md`. Refresh before expecting `/lr:trilens-loop` to appear in an installed
plugin copy.

Repo migration required: **no** — the skill writes no files into agent repos; existing agent repos
auto-stamp on next boot.

After upgrading:

1. **Claude Code:** `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/lore-framework/` (targeted), or
   `rm -rf ~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (broader fallback). Exit the current session and start a new one.
2. **Cursor:** run `bash /path/to/lore-framework/scripts/cursor-refresh-plugin`, then start a fresh
   `cursor-agent --plugin-dir /path/to/lore-framework` session.
```
